Plan baseline: Pilot Rules #10 equal split by tenant-days (approved 2026-11-02); R2 automated cost export (D3)
Runtime evidence: no bills yet; whether a 300-seat tenant and a 20-seat tenant should pay the same share is unknown until real usage data exists
TODO draft: What: decide whether to weight the hosting-cost split (e.g. by bookable resources, page views or storage) after 3 months of real AWS bills. Why: an equal split makes small tenants subsidize large ones as the tenant mix diverges, which can feel unfair under a "real cost" promise. Pros: fairer charges, defensible to tenants. Cons: more complex invoices and a harder-to-explain formula; needs per-tenant usage metering (UsageRecord). Context: equal split ships in v1 (Pilot Rules #10, D3); CostPool and TenantCostShare already store per-tenant rows, so a weight column can be added later; start by comparing CloudFront and S3 usage per tenant from AWS cost-allocation tags. Effort: M. Priority: P3. Depends on: 3 months of production bills, cost-allocation tags per tenant.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R9 weighted split | open question | TODOS.md entry (P3), equal split in v1 | not tracked | build weighting in v1 |
| R1–R8 | approved | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
Question D10:
D10 — R9: Track revisiting the equal hosting-cost split as a TODO?
Project/branch/task: main, engineering review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (Open Question 4, Pilot Rules #10).
ELI10: Every tenant pays an equal share of the AWS bill, whether it's a 20-seat or a 300-seat space. That's simple and fine to start, but as tenants grow apart in size, small spaces start paying for big ones. Real bills over a few months will show whether that's actually happening.
Stakes if we pick wrong: either fairness complaints with no plan to address them, or building a complex formula before there's data to justify it.
Recommendation: A because real bills are needed to choose a fair weighting, and tracking it keeps the question from getting lost.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Add to TODOS.md (recommended)
  ✅ Keeps v1 billing simple while making sure the fairness question gets revisited
  ✅ The cost tables already store per-tenant rows, so adding a weight later is cheap
  ❌ Small tenants may pay slightly more than their real usage for the first months
B) Skip
  ✅ No extra tracking; equal split may simply stay acceptable
  ✅ One less item on the backlog
  ❌ The question can get lost until a tenant complains about fairness
C) Build it now
  ✅ Fairer charges from the first invoice
  ✅ No later migration of the billing formula
  ❌ Needs per-tenant usage metering now and guesses weights before any real data
Net: a tracked P3 follow-up against either losing the question or guessing weights now.
Header: Split TODO

B) Skip
Don't track it; Open Question 4 stays in the design doc only. Effort S (zero implementation work now). Low risk, may be forgotten.
C) Build it now
Add per-tenant usage metering and a weighted split to v1 billing. Effort M (human: ~1 week / CC: ~5h). Medium risk: weights chosen without data.

## What already exists

Nothing in-house: the repo has docs only (plus the vendored gstack). Reused managed services and libraries, none rebuilt: Razorpay Checkout/OAuth/Payment Links/Refunds, Amazon SES, CloudFront + KeyValueStore (pending D5 spike), RDS PostgreSQL 17 with RLS and btree_gist, Hangfire on PostgreSQL, ASP.NET Core Identity + OpenIddict, AWS Data Exports (CUR 2.0, D3), Testcontainers, Playwright, axe, NetArchTest. No shared-code extraction candidates (no existing callers).

## Diagrams

Hosting-cost billing (D2, D3):

```
  month M closes
        │
  AWS Data Exports (CUR 2.0, prod account) ──► S3 (daily refresh)
        │
  10th of M+1: worker job MonthlyHostingBilling
        ├─ bill/InvoiceID present? ── no ──► hold invoices, alert SSPL Finance, retry daily
        │ yes
        ├─ CostPool(M) = net cost (credits applied), service breakdown
        ├─ re-read M-1: changed since invoiced? ── yes ──► AdjustmentLine on this invoice
        ├─ counted tenants = Active/PastDue/Restricted any day in M, prorated by days
        ├─ TenantCostShare = pool × tenant_days / Σ tenant_days  (SSPL test/demo → SSPL)
        ├─ pilots in free period → share recorded, no ₹0 invoice (SSPL absorbs)
        └─ HostingInvoice (+GST) ──► payment link / mandate ──► dunning (BRD §7.3)
```

Tenant context and sweeps (D4, D8, D9):

```
  API request / job step
        │
  open explicit transaction ──► set_config('app.tenant_id', id, true)
        │                                  │
        │                     RLS: current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)
        │                                  ├─ set   → that tenant's rows only
        │                                  └─ unset → zero rows (fail closed)
  commit / rollback (setting ends with the transaction)

  Sweep (hold expiry 30 s / reminders 5 min / no-show 15 min)
        │
  role cowos_sweeper: SELECT id, tenant_id WHERE <predicate>   (partial index, D8)
        │
  for each row: per-tenant transaction as above (D4) ──► release / remind / mark no-show
```

Messaging wallet debit (D6):

```
  send(message, tenant, cost, kind)
        │
  UPDATE wallet SET balance = balance - cost
   WHERE tenant_id = :t AND balance - cost >= floor(kind)   -- 0 notify, −₹500 OTP
        ├─ 1 row  → insert WalletTransaction (same tx) → send via provider
        └─ 0 rows → notification: email only │ OTP: offer email OTP
```

Files needing inline ASCII comments when code exists: the RLS interceptor/middleware, the MonthlyHostingBilling job, the wallet debit service, and the tenant state machine (pilot transitions).

## Failure modes

| New path | Realistic production failure | Test coverage | Error handling | User sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CUR export → billing job | Export late or missing on the 10th | Edge case "bill not final" | Hold invoices, alert Finance, daily retry | Nothing wrong; invoice arrives later |
| Prior-month re-read | AWS credit applied in month +2 | Edge case "prior month changes" | AdjustmentLine on next invoice | A clear adjustment line |
| RLS transaction wrapper | A query runs outside the transaction | Edge case "no tenant setting" | Fail closed: zero rows | An empty result or a handled error, never another tenant's data |
| Pooled connection | Session-level SET introduced by mistake | Pooled-connection leak test + architecture ban | CI blocks the change | Nothing (caught before release) |
| Sweeper role | Role policy drifts and sees too much, or too little | Isolation test on the role; cross-tenant sweep test | Per-tenant processing still applies D4 | Holds released on time |
| Wallet debit | Burst of parallel OTP sends | Parallel-send concurrency test | Conditional UPDATE refuses; email OTP offered | "Get your code by email" |
| CloudFront routing | Functions/KVS unsupported on multi-tenant distributions | D5 spike exit criteria | Fall back to Caddy edge before build | Nothing (decided in sprint 0) |

Critical gaps (no test, no handling, silent): 0.

## Worktree parallelization strategy

| Step | Modules touched | Depends on |
|------|----------------|------------|
| S0 CloudFront spike (D5) | infra/ | — |
| S1 Tenancy, Identity, RLS wrapper, sweeper role (D4, D9) | src/Modules/Tenancy, src/Modules/Identity, src/Cowos.SharedKernel | — |
| S2 Test stack (D7) | tests/, CI workflows | — |
| S3 Booking, Payments, Invoicing, sweeps + indexes (D8) | src/Modules/Booking, Payments, Invoicing, Cowos.Worker | S1, S2 |
| S4 Hosting-cost billing + pilot state (D2, D3, F-C2) | src/Modules/PlatformBilling, src/Modules/Tenancy, infra/ (CUR export) | S1, S2 |
| S5 Notifications + wallet (D6) | src/Modules/Notifications | S1, S2 |
| S6 Sites, renderer, edge routing | src/Modules/Sites, Domains, renderer/, web/packages/themes | S0, S1 |
| S7 Pack docs update to hosting cost only (F2) | docs/requirements | — |

Parallel lanes: Lane A: S0 → S6 (infra/, sites). Lane B: S1 → S3 (shared Tenancy). Lane C: S2 (tests/). Lane D: S7 (docs). After S1 + S2 merge: launch S3, S4, S5 in parallel.
Execution order: launch A + B + C + D. Merge S1 and S2. Then launch S3, S4, S5. Merge S0 before S6 builds on its result.
Conflict flags: S4 and S1 both touch src/Modules/Tenancy (pilot fields), so S4 starts after S1 merges. S3, S4 and S5 all depend on the D4 transaction wrapper in SharedKernel; don't change it in those lanes.

# Design Review (plan-design-review, 2026-11-03)

Target: this design doc and the UI requirements in `docs/requirements/` (FR-MW, FR-TA, FR-TW, FR-BA, FR-EM, FR-SA). No DESIGN.md exists. Mockups were not generated (gstack designer has no OpenAI API key on this machine). Independent design subagent ran (single-model; Codex not installed).

Initial design completeness: 4/10.

## Approved design decisions

### Pass 1: Information architecture (3/10 → 7/10)

**1A Slot picker (booking app, FR-BA-012/013).** Date strip → duration chips (1h / 2h / half day / full day) → list of available start times as 44px rows ("10:00 – 12:00 · ₹1,416"). With "any room" selected, times merge across rooms; choosing a specific room is an optional step. Availability is shown as text ("2 left", "Sold out"), never colour alone. Replaces the per-unit free/busy grid.

**2A Checkout order (FR-BA-015/017, US-6.1/6.2, Flow 3).** Step 1 summary: slot, total, "Pay to <legal name>", GST collapsed to one tappable line ("incl. GST ₹216 ▸"), one-line cancellation policy. Step 2 phone OTP with privacy notice, unticked marketing consent and bill-to-company. Step 3 the 10-minute hold starts with a visible countdown and Razorpay opens. If the slot was taken during OTP, the next free times show inline. Mobile: sticky bottom bar with total and "Pay ₹…". US-6.1 changes to hold-after-OTP.

**3A Tenant website first-screen contract (all themes, FR-TA-023, FR-TW-009/011, FR-TA-156).** First viewport: logo, H1 with brand + area ("Coworking in Madhapur"), one primary CTA (Book or Tour; required by pre-publish checks FR-TA-041), one proof line (price from, or rating). Mobile: one floating Book bar with a WhatsApp icon, no stacked floating buttons. The offer banner appears only after the cookie choice.

**4A Onboarding wizard order (FR-TA-001, FR-TA-008, FR-TA-007).** Goal → business/GSTIN → location → theme (previewed with the owner's logo, uploaded photos and demo spaces) → branding → inventory → policies → payments (MFA set up inline) → preview → publish. Demo inventory (FR-TA-008) becomes Must, labelled "Sample" with one-click remove; pre-publish checks block publishing with sample items.

**5A Billing page (FR-TA-175, replaces FR-TA-176 plan UI).** 1) Next invoice: issue date (10th), last month's amount, due date, any adjustment line; pilots see "Free until <date> · SSPL covered ₹X this month". 2) "0% commission: ₹0 on ₹<bookings> of bookings". 3) Messaging wallet, with a warning when customers are on email-only notifications. 4) AWS breakdown by service category plus tenant count, never other tenants' usage. No plan, limit or upgrade UI. *(Superseded in part by revision 2 and CEO review 3: invoices are issued on the 1st, there are no pilots, and the Owner chooses and switches between the Fixed and Usage plans (Rule #12, FR-TA-182); see FR-TA-175 for the current page.)*

**6A Front-desk Today view (FR-TA-091/096).** First: "Now / next" timeline of today's arrivals with a Check-in button per row. Always visible: Scan QR and "Walk-in day pass + cash" (one screen, about 10 seconds). Below: exceptions strip (requests with SLA countdown, unpaid bookings). Designed for 390px phones first; two columns on desktop. Visitor count moves to the dashboard.

**7A Builder layout (FR-TA-023/027/040).** Desktop: left section list (show/hide, drag to reorder, locked positions shown with a lock icon), centre live preview with device toggles, right form for the selected section; clicking a section in the preview selects it. Persistent "Draft saved · Not live" chip beside Publish. Tablet: the form becomes a slide-over.

**8A Domain setup (FR-TA-051/052, US-3.3).** Top: one status line ("Waiting for 2 of 3 records · checked 2 min ago · Check now"). Then registrar-specific steps with copy buttons. Before www is changed, warn if the domain already serves a website and offer `book.<domain>` instead. Detect Cloudflare proxying and say to turn it off. MFA setup appears inline if not done. Troubleshooting last.

Remaining Pass 1 gaps: booking app home hierarchy (Pass 4), owner landing after login, publish screen states (Pass 2), Super Admin and marketing-site navigation maps.

### Pass 2: Interaction states (1/10 → 7/10)

**9A Payment states (booking app, FR-BA-017/018/019).** Availability loads with a skeleton. After payment: "Confirming your payment…" with progress, polling up to 2 minutes, then "We've got your payment, confirmation is on its way by WhatsApp"; never an error while the webhook is pending. A resumable booking URL makes a UPI return or a reload land on the right state. Hold expired with the slot still free: one-tap retry. Slot lost after payment: "Full refund of ₹X started, 5–7 working days" plus the next free times. Invoice "being prepared" placeholder on the confirmation screen. Gateway down: offer Request-to-Book. Test mode and preview carry an unmistakable ribbon.

**10A Paused and suspended sites (US-9.3, FR-TW-012, Pilot Rules #6).** While Restricted, every Book CTA on the website switches to "Call / WhatsApp to book", with no billing mention to visitors; the booking app home shows the same contact options, and existing customers still see their bookings and invoices. Suspended: a page in the tenant's theme with name, address, phone, WhatsApp, "Online booking is temporarily unavailable" and the badge. The switch happens through a site rebuild or edge flag within minutes of the state change.

**11A Publish and pilot states (FR-TA-041/042/044, Pilot Rules #1/#9).** Pre-publish checks as a pass/fail list with Fix links; "Building… usually under 2 min"; Live with link and share kit; first publish "In review · typical under 4 working hours · you can keep editing" with the share-preview link; changes requested (reviewer notes + Fix links); render failed (retry, previous version stays live); rollback in progress → "v7 is live". Pilots: "Your site is ready. Publishing opens when Cowos launches", and a first-login handoff screen: "Review what we set up" plus a checklist of the owner's remaining steps (Razorpay, GSTIN/PAN, DNS, ToS/DPA).

**12A Billing states (5A billing page).** Each state has a one-line reason and one action: invoice held ("Waiting for AWS to finalize October's bill, expected by the 12th"); adjustment line ("+₹42: AWS corrected October on 6 Nov"); wallet below zero ("Customers now get email-only notifications · Top up"); pilot fair-use cap reached; end-of-pilot notices at 60/30/7 days with the expected first charge; Restricted ("Online booking paused until <invoice> is paid · Pay now"). An admin-wide banner appears only when the owner must act.

**13A Empty states.** Booking app: no slots on a date shows "Fully booked on <day> · next free: <day time>" with one tap; closed or holiday says why ("Closed for Diwali"). Admin lists (bookings, customers, leads, payments, invoices): a one-line context plus the primary action ("No leads yet · Share your tour link"; "No bookings yet · Copy your booking link"). Website: a section whose content is empty hides itself.

State table (what the user sees):

```
  FEATURE            | LOADING            | EMPTY                       | ERROR                          | SUCCESS                  | PARTIAL
  -------------------|--------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------|---------------------------
  Slot picker        | skeleton rows      | "Fully booked · next free"  | "Couldn't load times · Retry"  | times list               | closed/holiday reason
  Checkout + pay     | Razorpay opening   | n/a                         | payment failed → retry         | confirmation + QR        | "Confirming your payment…"
  Confirmation       | n/a                | n/a                         | n/a                            | QR, .ics, invoice        | invoice "being prepared"
  Website Book CTA   | n/a                | section hides               | n/a                            | Book → booking app       | Restricted → Call/WhatsApp
  Publish            | "Building… <2 min" | n/a                         | render failed, old version live| Live + share kit         | In review / changes asked
  Billing page       | skeleton           | trial: "first invoice <date>"| invoice held (reason)         | next invoice, paid       | adjustment line, wallet < 0
  Admin lists        | skeleton           | context + primary action    | "Couldn't load · Retry"        | rows                     | filters with zero matches
```

Remaining Pass 2 gaps: refund-failed alert screen for staff, Razorpay KYC pending wait, email-OTP fallback screen, stale website price during refresh.

### Pass 3: User journey (3/10 → 8/10)

```
  OWNER (Ravi)
  STEP | USER DOES                      | USER FEELS                   | PLAN SPECIFIES
  -----|--------------------------------|------------------------------|------------------------------------------
  1    | Signs up, trial starts         | Hopeful                      | US-1.1
  2    | GSTIN, location, hours         | "This is admin"              | GSTIN autofill (FR-TA-003)
  3    | Sees theme with own logo       | Excited: "that's mine"       | 4A
  4    | Connects Razorpay (leaves app) | Anxious: KYC can take days   | gap: KYC-pending wait
  5    | Publishes, waits for review    | Limbo                        | 11A: time expectation, keep editing
  6    | Site live, share kit           | Relief, pride                | 11A, Flow 1
  7    | First online booking arrives   | "It works, and it's mine"    | 14A
  8    | Sees billing mid-month         | Wary of hidden costs         | 5A, 12A, 15A estimate

  CUSTOMER (Arjun)
  1    | Lands on tenant site (phone)   | "Is there a seat today?"     | 3A first screen
  2    | Taps Book → booking app        | Expects the same brand       | 16A theme booking shell
  3    | Picks time, OTP, pays by UPI   | Wants speed                  | 1A, 2A, 9A
  4    | Arrives, shows pass at desk    | Reassured, quick             | 17A full-screen pass
```

**14A First-booking moment.** On the tenant's first paid online booking: a one-time WhatsApp message and admin dashboard card, "Your first online booking: ₹1,416 straight to your bank. Cowos took ₹0.", with a one-tap share of their booking link. Shown once, dismissible.

**15A Charge estimate on the billing page.** "This month so far: about ₹<low>–<high> · estimate, final on the 10th", from AWS month-to-date estimated charges and the current counted-tenant number. Pilots see "After your free year: about ₹X/month at today's numbers" from the 60-day notice onward.

**16A Theme booking shell (08 §9 changes).** Each theme package exports a booking shell: header and footer matching the website, the theme's type scale and fonts, and a product hero-image treatment. Booking screens (slot picker, checkout, account) stay one shared component set. Theme visual-regression tests cover the booking shell.

**17A Desk pass.** Confirmation messages link to a full-screen booking pass (large QR, customer name, date/time, room or seat, tenant logo) via a signed link that works without login and expires at booking end; cached for offline once opened, with an "Add to Home Screen" hint.

Remaining Pass 3 gap: the Razorpay KYC-pending wait (owner step 4).

### Pass 4: AI slop risk (3/10 → 6/10)

Classifier: tenant websites and the Cowos marketing site are PERSUADE; the booking app, Tenant Admin and Super Admin are OPERATE.

**18A Hero over owner photos (FR-TA-023, assumption A4).** Each theme declares its hero as either text-over-image with an enforced solid scrim (opacity computed so headline text reaches 4.5:1 on the image's sampled luminance; no gradients) or a split layout where text sits beside the photo. Owners set a focal point for cropping. Video heroes get a pause control and a poster image.

**19A Booking app home (FR-BA-010).** Location name (selector if more than one), then "Today: <n> seats left · Day pass ₹<price> · Book" (or the tenant's top product when there is no day pass), then meeting rooms each with their next free time, then other categories as a plain list. No category tiles or card grid. When every product is Enquire, "Book a tour" is the first row.

**20A Gallery and testimonials.** Gallery shows the owner's first 6–9 photos in a grid (owner sets the order), with "See all <n> photos" opening a keyboard- and swipe-friendly lightbox with alt text. Testimonials show up to 3 static quotes with name, role and company. No autoplay carousels in any theme.

**21A Section jobs and word limits (FR-TA-023, FR-TA-029).** Each section form shows its one job and a live word counter: Hero H1 ≤12 words (where + for whom), sub-line ≤20; About ≤60 words (what's different here); Amenities as short labels; CTA band ≤8 words with one action; FAQ answers ≤40 words. The AI copy assistant follows the same limits and a banned-phrase list ("vibrant", "unlock", "all-in-one", "welcome to").

Hard rejections: "beautiful image with weak brand" and "strong headline with no clear action" resolved by 3A; "busy imagery behind text" by 18A; "generic card grid first impression" by 19A; "carousel with no purpose" by 20A; "sections repeating the same mood" by 21A. Still at risk: "app UI made of stacked cards" on the product page and My bookings.

Remaining Pass 4 gaps: product page and My bookings layout, litmus 2/6/7 (visual anchor, motion, premium without shadows) pending the visual system.

### Pass 5: Design system (1/10 → 6/10)

**22A DESIGN.md in Sprint 0.** Run /design-consultation during Sprint 0 to produce DESIGN.md with the platform UI type scale, spacing scale, colour roles and component vocabulary (shadcn/ui on Radix) for Tenant Admin, Super Admin and the booking app, plus the contract every tenant theme must meet (tokens, 3A first-screen rules, 16A booking shell). It is a Sprint-0 exit criterion with a named owner, alongside the 2 theme designs.

**23A Colour roles (FR-TA-022).** From each owner colour, derive roles in OKLCH for light and dark mode: primary, primary-hover, primary-pressed, tint (backgrounds), border, link, focus ring, text-on-primary. Check every role in both modes (4.5:1 text, 3:1 UI components). A failing role adjusts lightness automatically and the owner sees a before/after. Selection colour, caret, scrollbars and focus rings use the derived palette.

**24A Four named theme directions (FR-TA-020, risk R16).** Loft (warehouse/industrial, bold condensed display), Metro (tech-park corporate, crisp sans, dense), Courtyard (calm, plant-filled, generous spacing, warm humanist type), Campus (startup/community, energetic, photo-led). Each has its own type pair, hero style (scrim or split, 18A) and density. The brief rules out the cream-serif, black-neon and broadsheet-hairline looks. Validate with 3 pilot owners before building themes 3–4.

**25A Fonts (FR-TA-022, NFR-PERF-03).** Each theme ships 2 named font pairs chosen in DESIGN.md (no Inter, Roboto, Arial or system as the display face). Every pair has a matching Noto or Anek family for Telugu and Devanagari, loaded only when the page contains that script. Self-hosted WOFF2, Latin subset by default, `font-display: swap`; fonts count against the 300 KB page budget.

Remaining Pass 5 gaps: DESIGN.md itself (concrete type and spacing scales, component list) is Sprint-0 work, not yet written.

### Pass 6: Responsive and accessibility (4/10 → 8/10)

**26A Admin on phones.** Designed for 390px phones: Today view, QR check-in, bookings list and detail, request approve/decline, leads, customers, payments, record offline payment, notifications. Desktop and tablet only: builder, theme and branding, domain setup, reports, invoice settings; on phones these show live/draft status read-only with "Best on a larger screen". Breakpoints: 390 / 768 / 1280. *(Exception: the seat map builder works fully on phones, Design Review 3, DR3-11A.)*

**27A Booking control patterns (NFR-A11Y-03/04/06).** Start times are a labelled radio group (each option reads "10:00 to 12:00, ₹1,416, available"); arrow keys move, Enter or Space selects, focus is visible. The hold countdown is visible every second, but an `aria-live` region announces only at 5 minutes, 1 minute and expiry. OTP is one input with `autocomplete="one-time-code"` and `inputmode="numeric"` (no split boxes), with errors linked via `aria-describedby`. Every booking page has landmarks; NVDA and TalkBack pass on the booking flow each release.

Remaining Pass 6 gap: keyboard alternative to drag-to-reorder in the builder section list.

### Pass 7: Unresolved design decisions (6 resolved, 0 deferred)

**28A Pricing page and trial (FR-MW-002/003/004, Flow 1, US-9.1, FR-SA-020/021).** One headline: "0% commission. You pay only your share of our real AWS bill", with a live "about ₹X per space per month right now" (last month's actual share, plus the last 3 months), what's included (website, booking app, hosting, SSL, backups, updates), messaging at cost with per-message prices, and the savings calculator. Sign-up → trial → "Add a payment method" to go Active; no plan picker. Super Admin plan screens become cost-pool, invoice and billing-run screens. *(Superseded in part by revisions of 2026-11-04: the page shows the rate card, sample bills (DR2-2A) and, since revision 2, the Fixed and Usage plans (FR-MW-002); "no plan picker" no longer holds.)*

**29A Customer-facing voice.** Every customer-facing screen, error, email, SMS and WhatsApp speaks as the tenant ("Workloft couldn't load times · Try again"), with the tenant's phone/WhatsApp as the help route. "Cowos" appears only in the badge and "Powered by" lines. DESIGN.md copy rules: utility language, no blame, one next action per error.

**30A Badge (BR-07, FR-TW-003, FR-BA-043).** Text link "Powered by Cowos" with a 12px mark as the last line of every website footer and under the booking app's pay bar and confirmation. Uses the theme's footer text colour at ≥4.5:1, light and dark versions, minimum 44px tap height, rendered by the platform so themes and custom CSS can't hide it, links to `cowos.in/?ref=<slug>`.

**31A Landing after login.** Owner before first publish → setup checklist; after first publish → dashboard (checklist leftovers become one card). Manager → dashboard filtered to their locations. Staff → Today view. All three stay one tap away in the nav.

**32A Product page and My bookings (FR-BA-011, FR-BA-030).** Product page: swipeable photo strip; title, capacity and "from ₹600/hour + GST" on one line; amenities as one row of labelled icons; one-line policy summary; sticky "Check times" bar. My bookings: a "Next up" row on top (date, time, room, Show pass), then Upcoming and Past as plain rows with status text; cancel and reschedule live in the booking detail. No card stacks.

**33A Razorpay KYC wait (FR-TA-130, assumption A1).** The payments step shows "Razorpay: waiting for approval (usually 1–5 days)" with a daily status check. The owner can continue and publish with Book a tour / Request to Book and offline payment; Instant Book products show as "Request" until Razorpay is live. On activation, the owner gets a one-tap "Turn on instant booking".

Re-rated after Pass 7: Pass 1 7 → 8 (31A), Pass 3 8 → 9 (33A), Pass 4 6 → 7 (32A; no hard rejection left at risk; litmus 2/6/7 wait on DESIGN.md).

# Engineering Re-review (plan-eng-review, 2026-11-03, after design review)

Target: this design doc, focused on the Design Review decisions 1A–33A. First eng review decisions (D2–D10 there) stand.

## Re-review scope record

feature answers: none proposed (design decisions 1A–33A approved); structure: A Original arrangement (re-review D1, answered 2026-11-03); accepted scope: design decisions 1A–33A built with separate services PassLinkService (17A), ResumeLinkService (9A), ChargeEstimateService (15A), TenantStateCtaService (10A) and ThemeShellLoader (16A); pending remedies: R10, R11, R12, R13, R14, R15

Scope Challenge result: scope accepted as-is.

Reality: PLAN_REALITY phase=eng rows=7/7 missing=none verdict=complete; unverified premises=2 (bundle budget with theme shells, public price exposure); FALSE premise: offline pass caching in Phase 1 (service worker is Phase 2, 08-architecture.md:239).

## Re-review decision ledger

### R14: offline desk pass in Phase 1 vs the Phase-2 service worker
Finding: 1, P2, confidence 9/10, docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md:1222 with docs/requirements/08-architecture.md:239, reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review
Plan baseline: 17A "cached for offline once opened, with an Add to Home Screen hint" (design review, 2026-11-03); service worker and web app manifest scheduled for Phase 2 PWA (08:239, 12-release-plan.md:23)
Runtime evidence: no code; offline caching of a page requires a service worker
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R14 offline pass | promised in 17A, no mechanism in Phase 1 | Phase 1 ships a minimal service worker scoped to the pass route only (caches the opened pass page and its QR image); full PWA stays Phase 2 | Phase 1 pass works online only; offline caching and Add to Home Screen move to Phase 2 with the PWA | move the whole PWA (manifest, shell caching, push) into Phase 1 |
| R14 Add to Home Screen hint | 17A | Phase 2 (needs manifest) | Phase 2 | Phase 1 |
| 17A signed link, layout | approved | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
| R10–R13, R15 | pending | pending | pending | pending |
Question D2:
D2 — R14: Should the desk pass work offline in Phase 1?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 17A, 08-architecture.md §9 Phase 2 PWA).
ELI10: The design says the desk pass keeps working with no signal once opened. That needs a small background script in the browser (a service worker), which the plan only adds in Phase 2 with the full installable app. Coworking spaces often have weak signal at the entrance, which is exactly where the pass is shown.
Stakes if we pick wrong: either the pass fails at the door on bad signal, or Phase 1 grows to include the whole installable-app work.
Recommendation: A because a tiny service worker limited to the pass page gives offline passes now without pulling the whole app-install project forward.
Completeness: A=9/10, B=6/10, C=10/10
Pros / cons:
A) Pass-only service worker (recommended)
  ✅ The opened pass, QR included, shows at the door even with no signal
  ✅ Small, scoped script; the full installable app stays in Phase 2 as planned
  ❌ A service worker in Phase 1 means cache-update and versioning rules from day one
B) Online-only pass in Phase 1
  ✅ No service worker until Phase 2; simplest Phase 1
  ✅ Pass still opens in one tap from WhatsApp when there is signal
  ❌ Fails at entrances with poor signal; 17A's offline promise slips to Phase 2
C) Full PWA in Phase 1
  ✅ Offline pass, Add to Home Screen and push notifications all at launch
  ✅ No second rollout of service-worker work later
  ❌ Pulls a Phase 2 project into the already tight 5-6 month MVP
Net: a small scoped script now against a pass that fails on weak signal or a bigger Phase 1.
Header: Offline pass
Options:
A) Pass-only service worker (recommended)
Phase 1 registers a service worker scoped to the pass route only, caching the opened pass page and QR image; cache cleared when the booking ends. Add to Home Screen and the full PWA stay in Phase 2. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low-medium risk.
B) Online-only pass in Phase 1
The pass works online only in Phase 1; offline caching and Add to Home Screen ship with the Phase 2 PWA. Effort S (zero implementation work now). Medium risk: weak-signal entrances.
C) Full PWA in Phase 1
Move the manifest, shell caching, offline pass and web push into Phase 1. Effort M (human: ~1 week / CC: ~1 day). Medium risk: MVP schedule.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Pass-only service worker (recommended), re-review D2 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Phase 1 registers a service worker scoped to the pass route only, caching the opened pass page and QR image, cleared when the booking ends; Add to Home Screen and the full PWA stay in Phase 2; 17A wording updated to drop the Add to Home Screen hint from Phase 1
History: none

### R10: privacy and security of the no-login desk pass link
Finding: A1, P1, confidence 8/10, docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md:1222, reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review
Plan baseline: 17A signed link without login, shows customer name, expires at booking end (design review, 2026-11-03); re-review D1 separate PassLinkService; R14 pass-only service worker (D2)
Runtime evidence: no code; NFR-PRIV-12 forbids personal data in URLs; DPDP makes the tenant the fiduciary for customer data
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R10 data on pass | full customer name, date/time, room | first name + last initial, date/time, room, tenant logo | full name as in 17A |
| R10 link token | signed, expires at booking end | opaque random token (no personal data in URL), HMAC-bound to booking id + purpose "pass", expires at booking end, revoked on cancel or reschedule | signed token, expires at booking end |
| R10 QR payload | unspecified | separate short-lived check-in code, not the link token; staff scan validates it against the tenant (FR-TA-096) | the link token itself |
| R10 headers | unspecified | noindex, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, Cache-Control private | unspecified |
| R10 tests | none | expired, revoked, wrong-tenant and wrong-purpose tokens rejected; forwarded link shows no full name | expired token rejected |
| R14 offline | approved (D2) | unchanged | unchanged |
| R11–R13, R15 | pending | pending | pending |
Question D3:
D3 — R10: How much should the no-login desk pass show, and how is it protected?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 17A, NFR-PRIV-12, FR-TA-096).
ELI10: The desk pass opens from a WhatsApp link without logging in, so anyone the link is forwarded to can open it. If it shows the customer's full name and the QR is the same secret as the link, a forwarded or leaked link gives a stranger personal details and a working check-in code.
Stakes if we pick wrong: a leaked link exposes a customer's details and lets someone else check in, a DPDP problem for the tenant.
Recommendation: A because a short name, an opaque revocable link and a separate check-in code keep the one-tap pass while limiting what a leak exposes.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=5/10
Pros / cons:
A) Minimal data, separate QR (recommended)
  ✅ Pass shows first name + last initial; the link carries no personal data and dies on cancel or reschedule
  ✅ QR holds a separate check-in code that staff validate against their own tenant
  ❌ Staff see a shortened name on the customer's phone and may need the booking list to confirm identity
B) Full name, link = QR
  ✅ Simplest: one token for the link and the QR, full name shown
  ✅ Staff read the full name straight off the pass
  ❌ A forwarded link exposes the full name and a working check-in code
Net: slightly less on the pass against what a leaked link gives away.
Header: Pass privacy
Options:
A) Minimal data, separate QR (recommended)
Pass shows first name + last initial, date/time, room and tenant logo. Link token is opaque (no personal data in the URL), HMAC-bound to booking id + purpose "pass", expires at booking end, revoked on cancel or reschedule. QR holds a separate short-lived check-in code validated against the tenant at scan. Headers: noindex, no-referrer, private cache. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Full name, link = QR
Pass shows the full name; the signed link token is also the QR payload; expires at booking end. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Medium risk: leaks via forwarded links.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Minimal data, separate QR (recommended), re-review D3 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: pass shows first name + last initial, date/time, room and tenant logo; link token opaque with no personal data in the URL, HMAC-bound to booking id + purpose pass, expires at booking end, revoked on cancel or reschedule; QR holds a separate short-lived check-in code validated against the tenant at scan; headers noindex, Referrer-Policy no-referrer, Cache-Control private; tests reject expired, revoked, wrong-tenant and wrong-purpose tokens and confirm a forwarded link shows no full name; 17A wording updated
History: none

### R11: how website Book buttons switch when a tenant becomes Restricted
Finding: A2, P2, confidence 7/10, docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md:1170, reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review
Plan baseline: 10A "through a site rebuild or edge flag within minutes of the state change" (design review, 2026-11-03); re-review D1 separate TenantStateCtaService; static sites rendered at publish (08 §8)
Runtime evidence: no code; CloudFront Functions can't read or rewrite response bodies, so static HTML changes only by re-rendering or by script in the page; state changes come from the daily dunning job (BRD §7.3) and payments
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R11 mechanism | "rebuild or edge flag" | TenantStateCtaService enqueues a data-refresh render of the live version on entering or leaving Restricted/Suspended, at lower priority than owner publishes | a small inline script on every page fetches /_state.json (from KVS at the edge) and swaps Book CTAs |
| R11 timing | "within minutes" | ≤2 min p95 (NFR-PERF-08 render budget) | on next page view |
| R11 no-JS / SEO | n/a | correct HTML with JS off; crawlers see the right CTA | crawlers and no-JS users still see Book |
| R11 per-view cost | n/a | none | one extra request per page view |
| R11 tests | none | state change → render job → page shows Call/WhatsApp; leaving Restricted restores Book | script swaps CTAs from state JSON |
| R10, R14 | approved (D3, D2) | unchanged | unchanged |
| R12, R13, R15 | pending | pending | pending |
Question D4:
D4 — R11: How should the website's Book buttons switch when online booking pauses?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 10A, 08-architecture.md §6/§8).
ELI10: Tenant websites are pre-built pages on a CDN. When a tenant's online booking pauses, every "Book" button should become "Call / WhatsApp to book". Either Cowos re-builds that tenant's pages when the state changes (a few a day), or each page runs a tiny script that checks the state every time someone visits.
Stakes if we pick wrong: either visitors keep clicking Book into a dead end, or every page view of every tenant makes an extra request.
Recommendation: A because state changes are rare, a rebuild keeps pages correct without JavaScript, and it reuses the publish pipeline.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Re-render on state change (recommended)
  ✅ Pages are correct as plain HTML, for visitors with JS off and for search engines
  ✅ No extra request per page view; reuses the existing render pipeline at low priority
  ❌ Takes up to ~2 minutes after the state changes, not instant
B) Client script checks state
  ✅ Switches on the very next page view, no rebuild needed
  ✅ No render-queue load on dunning days
  ❌ One extra request per page view for every tenant, and JS-off visitors still see Book
Net: a short delay after a rare event against an extra request on every page view.
Header: CTA switching
Options:
A) Re-render on state change (recommended)
TenantStateCtaService enqueues a data-refresh render of the tenant's live site version whenever it enters or leaves Restricted or Suspended, at lower priority than owner publishes; pages are correct within ~2 min p95. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Client script checks state
Every tenant page includes a small inline script that fetches /_state.json (served from KeyValueStore at the edge) and swaps Book CTAs to Call/WhatsApp when Restricted. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Medium risk: per-view request, no-JS gap.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Re-render on state change (recommended), re-review D4 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: TenantStateCtaService enqueues a data-refresh render of the tenant's live site version whenever it enters or leaves Restricted or Suspended, at lower priority than owner publishes; pages correct within ~2 min p95; tests: state change triggers render and page shows Call/WhatsApp, leaving Restricted restores Book; 10A wording updated to re-render only
History: none

### R12: how the marketing site gets its "about ₹X per space" number
Finding: A3, P2, confidence 7/10, docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md:1264, reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review
Plan baseline: 28A live per-space share plus last 3 months on the pricing page (design review, 2026-11-03); marketing site pre-rendered (08 §3, FR-MW-009); billing run on the 10th (first eng review D2/D3)
Runtime evidence: no code; the share changes once a month after the billing run; a public live endpoint would expose platform cost data on every request
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R12 data path | unspecified ("live") | after each monthly billing run, write a small published JSON (per-space average share for last month + previous 2 months) and rebuild the marketing pricing page from it | public API endpoint /api/v1/public/pricing/current read by the page in the browser |
| R12 what is public | unspecified | per-space average share only; never the total AWS bill, tenant count or any tenant's data | whatever the endpoint returns (risk of total and count) |
| R12 freshness | "right now" | updated monthly, labelled "last month's actual" | per request |
| R12 failure mode | n/a | page keeps last published number | page shows no price if API fails |
| R12 tests | none | billing run writes JSON; pricing page renders it; JSON contains no total or count | endpoint returns expected fields |
| R10, R11, R14 | approved (D3, D4, D2) | unchanged | unchanged |
| R13, R15 | pending | pending | pending |
Question D5:
D5 — R12: How should the pricing page get its "about ₹X per space" number?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 28A, FR-MW-009 pre-rendered marketing site).
ELI10: The pricing page shows what a space actually paid last month. That number only changes once a month, after billing on the 10th. It can be baked into the page each month, or fetched live from a public API. A live public API also risks revealing SSPL's total AWS bill or how many tenants it has.
Stakes if we pick wrong: either the pricing page breaks when an API is down, or business numbers leak to anyone who looks.
Recommendation: A because a monthly number baked into the page is always available, fast, and shows only the per-space amount.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Publish monthly, baked in (recommended)
  ✅ Billing run writes the per-space figure; the static pricing page always shows it, even if the API is down
  ✅ Only the per-space average is public, never the total bill or tenant count
  ❌ The figure updates once a month, so it's "last month's actual", not "right now"
B) Public live API
  ✅ Page always shows the newest figure without a rebuild
  ✅ Could show more detail later from the same endpoint
  ❌ A public cost endpoint on every page view; risk of exposing totals; no price if it fails
Net: a monthly, safe number against a live endpoint with leak and uptime risk.
Header: Price number
Options:
A) Publish monthly, baked in (recommended)
After each monthly billing run, write a small JSON with the per-space average share for last month and the two before it, then rebuild the marketing pricing page from it. The JSON never contains the total AWS bill, tenant count or any tenant's data; the page keeps the last published number if a run fails. 28A wording becomes "last month's actual". Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Public live API
Add /api/v1/public/pricing/current returning the current per-space share and history, fetched by the pricing page in the browser. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Medium risk.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Publish monthly, baked in (recommended), re-review D5 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: after each monthly billing run, write a JSON with the per-space average share for last month and the two before it and rebuild the marketing pricing page from it; the JSON never contains the total AWS bill, tenant count or any tenant's data; the page keeps the last published number if a run fails; 28A wording becomes last month's actual; tests: billing run writes JSON, page renders it, JSON has no total or count

Question D6:
D6 — R15: Where should the mid-month charge estimate come from?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 15A, first eng review D3).
ELI10: The billing page shows "about ₹1,300–1,600 this month so far". That can be worked out once a day from the AWS cost data Cowos already imports, or fetched from AWS's Cost Explorer every time an owner opens the page. AWS charges for each Cost Explorer call, and the number only really changes once a day.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Cowos pays AWS on every page view and the page is slower, or the estimate is stale by up to a day.
Recommendation: A because the daily cost import already has the numbers, so a once-a-day calculation is free to read and fast.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Daily from imported costs (recommended)
  ✅ Computed once a day from data Cowos already imports; reading it costs nothing
  ✅ Fast page loads; testable with fixture cost files
  ❌ Up to a day old; the range is Cowos's own projection, not AWS's forecast
B) Cost Explorer per view
  ✅ Uses AWS's own forecast, always current to AWS's latest data
  ✅ No projection logic to write
  ❌ About $0.01 per page view, slower page, and one more AWS dependency at view time
Net: a free, daily estimate against a per-view AWS charge.
Header: Estimate source
Options:
A) Daily from imported costs (recommended)
ChargeEstimateService runs once a day after the CUR refresh: month-to-date pool plus a straight-line projection to month end, divided by today's counted tenants, stored as low/high on CostPool (high = projection + 15% buffer); the billing page reads the stored value. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Cost Explorer per view
ChargeEstimateService calls the AWS Cost Explorer API (with forecast) each time a billing page loads. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Medium risk: per-view cost and latency.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Daily from imported costs (recommended), re-review D6 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: ChargeEstimateService runs once a day after the CUR refresh: month-to-date pool plus a straight-line projection to month end, divided by today's counted tenants, stored as low/high on CostPool (high = projection + 15% buffer); the billing page reads the stored value; test with fixture CUR data

Question D7:
D7 — R16: Should the resumable booking link need the customer's login?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 9A, 2A OTP-before-pay, FR-BA-006 per-tenant sessions).
ELI10: After paying in a UPI app, the customer comes back to a link that shows "confirming your payment" or "confirmed". Because they already logged in with OTP before paying, that link can simply require their login. If they return in a different browser, they enter one OTP again. The alternative is a link that works for anyone who has it, like the desk pass.
Stakes if we pick wrong: either a forwarded or leaked link shows someone's booking and payment state, or some customers re-enter an OTP after paying.
Recommendation: A because the customer is already logged in at this point, so the session protects the link with nothing new to secure.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Needs login session (recommended)
  ✅ No new token type to secure; another person with the link sees nothing
  ✅ Same browser (the usual UPI return) lands straight on the right state
  ❌ Returning in a different browser asks for one more OTP before showing the state
B) Works without login
  ✅ Lands on the right state in any browser with no OTP
  ✅ Can be sent in the confirmation message too
  ❌ Another secret link to protect; anyone with it sees the booking and payment state
Net: an occasional extra OTP against another bearer link to secure.
Header: Resume link
Options:
A) Needs login session (recommended)
ResumeLinkService serves /book/checkout/{bookingRef} and a status endpoint that require the customer's session; without one the page asks for OTP and then shows the state; another customer's session gets 404. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Works without login
ResumeLinkService issues an opaque token (purpose "resume", expires 1 hour after hold creation) that opens the booking state without login, following the R10 token rules. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Medium risk.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Needs login session (recommended), re-review D7 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: ResumeLinkService serves /book/checkout/{bookingRef} and a status endpoint that require the customer's session; without one the page asks for OTP and then shows the state; another customer's session gets 404; tests for each case
History: none

| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R13 loading | unspecified | ThemeShellLoader dynamic-imports only the tenant's theme shell chunk, named in site-config (theme key + published version); core booking screens stay in the main bundle | all theme shells bundled into the main booking bundle; pick at runtime |
| R13 budget | 200 KB gzip initial | main bundle ≤200 KB; each shell chunk ≤40 KB gzip excluding fonts [estimated budget]; fonts per 25A | everything counts against 200 KB; grows with every new theme |
| R13 first paint | n/a | shell header/footer HTML inlined at the edge with site-config so no unbranded flash; JS chunk hydrates | immediate (already bundled) |
| R13 version pinning | n/a | shell chunk version matches the tenant's published theme version | always latest shell code |
| R13 tests | none | bundle-size check in CI for main + each shell chunk; tenant on theme X never downloads theme Y | bundle-size check for the main bundle |

Question D8:
D8 — R13: How should the booking app load each tenant's theme header and styling?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md (design 16A, NFR-PERF-04 200 KB bundle budget).
ELI10: The booking app is one app shared by every tenant, but each theme now supplies its own header, footer and type styles. If every theme's code ships in the one app, every customer downloads all of them, and the app grows with each new theme. Loading only the tenant's own theme piece keeps the app small.
Stakes if we pick wrong: the booking app gets slower on mid-range Android phones with every theme added, right where customers pay.
Recommendation: A because loading only the tenant's own theme shell keeps the 200 KB budget no matter how many themes exist (performance budgets are requirements).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=6/10
Pros / cons:
A) Load only the tenant's shell (recommended)
  ✅ Main bundle stays under 200 KB however many themes ship; each shell has its own size budget
  ✅ Shell version pinned to the tenant's published theme, so a theme update never surprises a live site
  ❌ One extra small download per visit, so the header HTML must be inlined to avoid a flash
B) Bundle all shells
  ✅ No extra request; the right shell is already in the bundle
  ✅ Simpler build and loading code
  ❌ Every customer downloads every theme; budget breaks as themes are added
Net: a small per-visit chunk against a bundle that grows with every theme.
Header: Theme shell
Options:
A) Load only the tenant's shell (recommended)
ThemeShellLoader dynamic-imports only the tenant's theme shell chunk named in site-config (theme key + published version); the edge inlines the shell's header/footer HTML with site-config to avoid an unbranded flash; CI checks main bundle ≤200 KB and each shell chunk ≤40 KB gzip (fonts excluded). Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Bundle all shells
Include every theme's booking shell in the single booking bundle and select it at runtime; CI checks the main bundle only. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Medium risk: budget erosion.

State: approved
Actual answer: A) Load only the tenant's shell (recommended), re-review D8 answered 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: ThemeShellLoader dynamic-imports only the tenant's theme shell chunk named in site-config (theme key + published version); the edge inlines the shell header/footer HTML with site-config to avoid an unbranded flash; CI checks main bundle <=200 KB and each shell chunk <=40 KB gzip (fonts excluded); test that a tenant on theme X never downloads theme Y

## Re-review: Diagrams

```
  Desk pass (17A, D2, D3)
  confirmation WhatsApp ──► /book/pass/<opaque token>
        │                          │
        │                 PassLinkService: token → booking (purpose=pass, not expired, not revoked)
        │                          ├─ invalid → "This pass is no longer valid · contact <tenant>"
        │                          └─ valid → pass page (first name + initial, time, room, logo)
        │                                     ├─ QR = short-lived check-in code (tenant-bound)
        │                                     └─ pass-only service worker caches page + QR
  front desk scan ──► /admin/check-ins/scan (code → booking, same tenant only) ──► Checked in

  UPI return (9A, D7)
  Razorpay → UPI app → browser ──► /book/checkout/<bookingRef>
        ├─ session present → ResumeLinkService → poll status (≤2 min) → Confirming / Confirmed / Refund started
        └─ no session → OTP → same state view

  Tenant state → website (10A, D4)
  dunning job / payment ──► Tenant.state change ──► TenantStateCtaService
        └─ enter/leave Restricted|Suspended → enqueue low-priority data-refresh render → S3 → KVS pointer → CTAs updated (≤2 min)

  Pricing number (28A, D5)            Estimate (15A, D6)
  billing run (10th) → pricing.json    daily CUR refresh → ChargeEstimateService
     → marketing rebuild                  → CostPool.estimate_low/high → billing page reads
```

## Re-review: Failure modes

| New path | Realistic production failure | Test coverage | Error handling | User sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Desk pass | Link forwarded to a stranger | Forwarded-link edge case | Minimal data on pass; QR tenant-bound | First name + initial only |
| Desk pass offline | Service worker caches a stale pass after reschedule | Revocation edge case | Cache cleared at booking end; revoked token returns not-found when online | "This pass is no longer valid" once online |
| UPI return | Return lands in a different browser | Different-browser edge case | OTP then state | One OTP prompt, then the right state |
| CTA switching | Render queue busy on a heavy publish day | Restricted critical path | Low-priority job retries; old CTAs stay until render | Book button for up to a few minutes |
| Pricing JSON | Billing run fails | Billing-run-fails edge case | Page keeps last published number | Last month's figure, still labelled |
| Charge estimate | CUR refresh late | Fixture-based estimate test | Shows previous day's stored estimate with its date | "Estimate as of <date>" |
| Theme shell | Shell chunk fails to load | Bundle/loader test | Inlined header/footer HTML stays; core booking still works | Branded header, booking unaffected |

Critical gaps (no test, no handling, silent): 0.

### CEO-S1: defer platform billing automation and non-pilot self-serve sign-up
Finding: no pilot pays anything for 12 months after first publish (Pilot Rules #3, #4), so the billing machinery has no paying user at launch except non-pilot sign-ups, whose demand is unverified (design:13-17).
Moves to the Billing release: MonthlyHostingBilling CUR 2.0 job, CostPool/TenantCostShare/HostingInvoice/AdjustmentLine, payment-link and UPI Autopay/e-mandate collection, dunning and automatic Restricted/Suspended moves (TenantStateCtaService, RT4), the pricing JSON (RT5), the daily charge estimate (RT6), the 14-day trial, non-pilot wallet top-up, public self-serve sign-up.
Stays at launch: tenant state model and Super Admin manual state change, pilot flag and free-period end date, SSPL-funded messaging wallet with atomic debits and the fair-use caps (Pilot Rules #5), a "request a pilot / join waitlist" form on the marketing site.
Unchanged: Pilot Rules #7 exit criterion (one real hosting charge paid and one dunning cycle run on production before the first pilot converts).
Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (defer) | B (keep) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Non-pilot sign-up opens | Pilot Rules #7 (approved 2026-11-02) | launch | Billing release (launch + 4 months) | launch |
| CUR billing, invoices, collection, dunning | eng D2/D3, RT4-RT6 (approved) | build by week 21 | build by launch + 4 months, same design | build by week 21 |

## currentDecision (CEO-S1)
Question: D2 — CEO-S1: Should platform billing and non-pilot self-serve sign-up move to a release 4 months after launch?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review (SCOPE REDUCTION) of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, Pilot Rules #7 and release plan M5.
ELI10: For the first year the 20 pilots pay nothing, so the code that reads the AWS bill, sends invoices, collects money and chases late payers has nobody to bill at launch except brand-new outside sign-ups we have no evidence for. Moving it to a release 4 months after launch frees about 3 weeks of two engineers before launch, right where the plan is most crowded. It still ships 8 months before the first pilot has to pay, and the "prove billing works first" rule stays.
Stakes if we pick wrong: defer and an outside space that wants to sign up at launch waits up to 4 months; keep and weeks 19-21 stay overloaded, squeezing hardening and the pen-test fixes the launch gate needs.
Recommendation: A because nobody pays during the free year, and the freed weeks protect the production entry gate, which blocks all 10 launch pilots.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Defer to the Billing release (recommended)
  ✅ Frees about 3 weeks of one backend and one frontend engineer before launch [estimated]
  ✅ Billing still ships about 8 months before the first pilot is invoiced, with the same design
  ❌ Non-pilot spaces can only join a waitlist until the Billing release, about 4 months after launch
B) Keep at launch
  ✅ Matches the approved Pilot Rules #7: any space in any city can sign up and pay from day one
  ✅ Billing gets real production use months earlier, from SSPL's own paid test tenant
  ❌ Weeks 19-21 carry billing, dunning, notifications, domains and Super Admin together
Net: earlier outside sign-ups vs a less crowded run-up to the launch gate.
Header: Billing timing
A) Defer to Billing release (recommended)
Move CUR billing, invoices, collection, dunning, pricing JSON, charge estimate, trial and public sign-up to a release at launch + 4 months; launch keeps the pilot free year, SSPL-funded wallet and a waitlist form. Effort S (zero implementation work now; spec edits human: ~2h / CC: ~10min). Low risk. ✅ Frees ~3 weeks of 2 engineers before launch ✅ Ships ~8 months before the first pilot invoice ❌ Outside spaces wait up to 4 months
B) Keep at launch
Keep platform billing, dunning and self-serve sign-up in Phase 1 by week 21 as approved in Pilot Rules #7. Effort S (zero implementation change to the plan). Medium risk: crowded weeks 19-21. ✅ Any space can sign up and pay from day one ✅ Billing proven on production earlier ❌ Weeks 19-21 stay overloaded

State: approved
Actual answer: D2 answered 'Defer to Billing release (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Defer to a Billing release at launch + 4 months [estimated]: CUR billing job and billing tables, payment-link and UPI Autopay/e-mandate collection, dunning and automatic Restricted/Suspended moves (RT4), pricing JSON (RT5), daily charge estimate (RT6), 14-day trial, non-pilot wallet top-up, public self-serve sign-up. Launch keeps the tenant state model with manual Super Admin state change, pilot flag and free-period end date, SSPL-funded wallet and caps, and a pilot request/waitlist form. Pilot Rules #7 changes: non-pilot sign-up and SSPL's paid test tenant start at the Billing release; its exit criterion is unchanged.
History: narrowed by CEO-A1 (D5, 2026-11-03): RT4 ships at launch for FR-SA-011 suspend/reinstate; only its dunning triggers stay in the Billing release

## currentDecision (CEO-S3)
Question: D4 — CEO-S3: Should Cowos launch with 2 themes and add themes 3-4 about 6 weeks after launch?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review (SCOPE REDUCTION) of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, design 24A and release plan M6.
ELI10: Themes 3 and 4 are scheduled for the last three weeks before launch, the same weeks the team fixes pen-test findings, runs the restore drill and takes 10 pilots live. The design already says to check the theme ideas with 3 pilot owners before building themes 3-4. Launching with 2 themes and adding the other 2 six weeks later takes them out of that crunch and lets real owners pick the directions.
Stakes if we pick wrong: defer and 20 pilots in one city choose from 2 themes at first, so some sites look alike until themes 3-4 land; keep and theme work competes with the launch gate in its last three weeks.
Recommendation: A because it moves design work out of the launch-gate weeks and follows 24A's own rule to validate themes 3-4 with pilot owners first.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Launch with 2 themes, add 3-4 six weeks later (recommended)
  ✅ Takes about 2 weeks of frontend and design work out of weeks 22-24, when the launch gate is decided
  ✅ Themes 3-4 get built after 3 pilot owners have reacted to the directions, as 24A asks
  ❌ Early pilots in one city share 2 themes; branding colours and photos are the only difference at first
B) Keep all 4 themes at launch
  ✅ Pilots pick from all four 24A directions from their first publish
  ✅ No second theme release to plan, test and announce after launch
  ❌ Theme design, build and accessibility passes run alongside pen-test fixes and pilot go-live
Net: more theme choice on day one vs a calmer final three weeks before the launch gate.
Header: Themes
A) Launch with 2 themes (recommended)
Ship Loft and Metro at launch (or the 2 directions pilot validation favours) with all 24A rules and RT7 budgets; build the other 2 after validation with 3 pilot owners, released about 6 weeks after launch, each passing the same performance and accessibility checks first. Effort S (zero implementation work now; spec edits human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Low risk. ✅ Frees ~2 weeks of FE + design in weeks 22-24 ✅ Themes 3-4 shaped by pilot feedback ❌ Early pilot sites share 2 themes
B) Keep all 4 themes at launch
Keep themes 3-4 in M6 (weeks 22-24) as planned, all 4 passing Lighthouse and accessibility checks before launch. Effort S (zero implementation change to the plan). Medium risk: competes with launch-gate work. ✅ Full theme choice from day one ✅ No later theme release ❌ Theme work runs alongside pen-test fixes and go-live

State: approved
Actual answer: D4 answered 'Launch with 2 themes (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Launch with 2 themes (Loft and Metro, or the 2 directions pilot validation favours) with all 24A rules, 23A tokens and RT7 loader/budgets. Build the other 2 after validating the directions with 3 pilot owners; release about 6 weeks after launch, each passing Lighthouse, axe and the screen-reader pass before release. Launch checklist item becomes 'Core Web Vitals and Lighthouse budgets met on every launched theme'.

Current scope: SCOPE REDUCTION (D1). Deferred to the Billing release: CEO-S1 (D2), CEO-S2 (D3). Deferred to launch + 6 weeks: CEO-S3 (D4). Proposal S4 (marketing extras) withdrawn: the savings calculator (FR-MW-004) and demo sites (FR-MW-007) serve pilot recruitment.

Dependent corrections (follow directly from D2-D4, no new behavior):
- The CEO-S1 pilot request form is the existing "Book a demo" form (FR-MW-008); the owner "left to do" list from CEO-S2 is the existing dashboard checklist (FR-TA-002).
- CEO-S2 defers only the optional GSTIN legal-name lookup; GSTIN format/checksum validation and PAN derivation (FR-TA-003) stay at launch because GST invoices depend on them. The subdomain slug (FR-TA-005) is set in settings.
- Launch defers FR-MW-005 sign-up, FR-MW-006 referral capture and FR-MW-012 slug check with CEO-S1. The pricing page (28A) shows the promise and the pilot offer at launch and adds "last month's actual" after the first billing run. Demo sites (FR-MW-007): one per launched theme.

### Section 1: Architecture

```
  LAUNCH (week 24)                                     BILLING RELEASE (launch + ~4 months)
  cowos.in (static) -- product pages, pricing promise, + FR-MW-005 sign-up, FR-MW-006 referrals,
                       demo sites (1 per theme),         pricing JSON (RT5), "last month's actual"
                       Book-a-demo / pilot request (MW-008)
  Super Admin ------- create pilot (Active+pilot flag), + dunning dashboard (SA-023), billing run
                       support grant (SA-030), suspend/    admin, invoices, FR-SA-051 metrics
                       reinstate (SA-011), pilot scorecard (CEO-A4)
  Tenant Admin ------ settings screens + checklist      + wizard 4A, GSTIN lookup, Places,
                       (TA-002), builder (2 themes),       charge estimate (RT6)
                       inventory, bookings, CRM
  Booking app / sites  as approved in eng + design reviews + Restricted CTA switch from dunning
  Worker ------------ renders, sweeps, notifications,   + MonthlyHostingBilling, dunning jobs
                       SSPL-funded wallet debits,
                       suspend render (CEO-A1)
```

Tenant states at launch (no billing events exist, so Trial, PastDue and Restricted have no trigger):
```
  [created by Super Admin] --> Active(pilot) --SA-011 suspend (reason, audited)--> Suspended
                                   ^                                                   |
                                   +------------ SA-011 reinstate (audited) -----------+
  Invalid at launch: any move into Trial / PastDue / Restricted (Super Admin offers no such action;
  the API rejects it with 409 until the Billing release enables dunning).
```

Finding A1 `[P1] (8/10)`: CEO-S1 deferred RT4 (TenantStateCtaService) with dunning, but FR-SA-011 suspension ships at launch and 10A requires the suspended page in the tenant's theme. RT4 renders at low priority, so an owner publish already in flight can finish after a suspension and put the normal site back live. Decision row CEO-A1.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO-A1, product owner (Section 1) | FR-SA-011 (04-srs:310); 10A (design:1170); R11/RT4 (design:1503, 1788); CEO-S1 accepted scope | RT4 deferred with dunning; manual suspension at launch has no site switch | Keep RT4 at launch for FR-SA-011 suspend/reinstate, with a state check at the live-pointer switch | approved | D5 answer 'Keep RT4 for suspend (recommended)', 2026-11-03; scope as in CEO-A1 Accepted scope |
