| Publish vs suspend race | none | unguarded | pointer switch re-reads tenant state in the same transaction; a normal-site render aborts if Suspended; suspended render aborts if no longer Suspended | unpublish flips pointer; owner publish blocked while Suspended |
| Dunning-driven Restricted CTAs | CEO-S1 (deferred) | Billing release | Billing release (unchanged) | Billing release |
| Tests | none | none | suspend shows suspended page within ~2 min; reinstate restores site; publish in flight then suspend leaves suspended page live (controlled pause) | unpublish shows not-available page; publish rejected while Suspended |

## currentDecision (CEO-A1)
Question: D5 — CEO-A1: When Super Admin suspends a tenant at launch, how should its website change?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review Section 1 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, FR-SA-011 and design 10A after CEO-S1.
ELI10: At launch SSPL can still suspend a space, for example for fraud or a legal request. The approved design says the space's website then shows a "booking temporarily unavailable" page with its contact details. The piece that swaps the site was moved to the Billing release along with late-payment handling, so right now a suspended space would keep its Book buttons. There's also a timing trap: if the owner was publishing at that moment, the publish could finish later and put the normal site back.
Stakes if we pick wrong: a suspended space keeps taking bookings, or its customers hit a dead page with no way to contact the space.
Recommendation: A because it keeps the approved 10A behavior at about a day of work and closes the publish race with an explicit state check (explicit over clever).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=6/10
Pros / cons:
A) Keep RT4 for manual suspend, with a state check (recommended)
  ✅ Suspended visitors see the 10A page with the space's phone and WhatsApp, as approved
  ✅ A publish that finishes after a suspension can no longer put the normal site back live
  ❌ About a day of backend work moves back before launch, out of the Billing release
B) Unpublish only at launch
  ✅ No render job at launch; unpublish just switches the live pointer to a platform page
  ✅ Blocking owner publishes while Suspended closes the race simply
  ❌ Visitors see a generic platform page without the space's contact details, against 10A
Net: about one day of work against keeping the approved suspended page and its contact details.
Header: Suspend page
A) Keep RT4 for suspend (recommended)
TenantStateCtaService runs at launch, triggered by FR-SA-011 suspend and reinstate; the live-pointer switch re-reads tenant state in the same transaction, so a normal-site render aborts if the tenant is Suspended and a suspended-page render aborts if it is not; owner publish is blocked while Suspended with a clear message. Tests: suspend shows the 10A page within ~2 min, reinstate restores the site, a paused in-flight publish released after suspension leaves the suspended page live. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Unpublish only at launch
FR-SA-011 suspend unpublishes the site (platform "not available" page) and blocks owner publishes while Suspended; the themed suspended page arrives with RT4 at the Billing release. Tests: suspend shows the platform page, publish rejected while Suspended. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk, but departs from 10A.

State: approved
Actual answer: D5 answered 'Keep RT4 for suspend (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: TenantStateCtaService (RT4) ships at launch, triggered by FR-SA-011 suspend and reinstate only (dunning triggers stay in the Billing release). The live-pointer switch re-reads tenant state in the same transaction: a normal-site render aborts if the tenant is Suspended; a suspended-page render aborts if it is not. Owner publish is blocked while Suspended with a clear message. Tests: suspend shows the 10A page within ~2 min; reinstate restores the site; a paused in-flight publish released after suspension leaves the suspended page live.
History: none

Finding A2 `[P1] (9/10)`, dependent on CEO-S1 (no question): with FR-MW-005 sign-up deferred, nothing creates a pilot tenant or its Owner account. Pilot Rules #4 (approved) already says pilots are created directly in Active with a pilot flag. Launch therefore needs a Super Admin "Create pilot tenant" action: business name, city, slug, pilot flag, then an Owner invite that reuses the FR-TA-172 invite flow with the Owner role (single-use link, 72-hour expiry, email verified by the link, phone verified by OTP on acceptance, password and MFA set by the owner, ToS and DPA accepted by the owner per Pilot Rules #9). The action is audited like FR-SA-011. Task CT2.

Other Section 1 checks: no new coupling beyond RT4's existing Sites-Tenancy link; the Billing release adds back the dunning triggers into the same service. Scaling: unchanged from the eng reviews (20 pilots is far below the 1,500-host spike target). Single points of failure: unchanged; the onboarding owner (Pilot Rules #9) is now the only path to a new tenant until the Billing release, which is a people SPOF noted in Section 8. Rollback: launch-only additions are a Super Admin action and a form; rolling back means disabling the action, no data migration.

### Section 2: Error and rescue map (new launch paths only; eng reviews cover the rest)

```
  METHOD/CODEPATH                         | WHAT CAN GO WRONG                          | EXCEPTION CLASS
  ----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------|--------------------------------
  TenantStateCtaService.OnSuspend/Reinstate| render job fails (renderer error, S3 write)| RenderFailedException
                                          | tenant state changed before pointer switch | RenderAbortedStateChangedException
  SiteRenderer.SwitchLivePointer          | owner publish while Suspended              | TenantSuspendedException (409)
  SuperAdmin.CreatePilotTenant            | slug taken                                 | SlugConflictException (409)
                                          | KVS host mapping write fails               | EdgeMappingException
  OwnerInvite.Accept                      | link expired / already used                | InviteExpiredException (410)
                                          | phone OTP fails                            | OtpVerificationException
  TenantAdmin.SetupChecklist (FR-TA-002)  | Razorpay status call times out             | GatewayStatusTimeoutException
                                          | DNS not yet propagated                     | (state, not error) DomainPending
  Marketing.BookDemo (FR-MW-008)          | bot flood, email notify fails              | see CEO-A2
  ----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------|--------------------------------

  EXCEPTION CLASS                    | RESCUED? | RESCUE ACTION                                   | USER SEES
  -----------------------------------|----------|-------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------
  RenderFailedException              | Y        | retry 3x with backoff, then alert Super Admin;   | normal site until success; Super Admin
                                     |          | tenant detail shows "suspended page not live"   | sees the alert and render state
  RenderAbortedStateChangedException | Y        | end job, log info with tenant, state, version   | correct page for the current state
  TenantSuspendedException           | Y        | reject publish                                  | owner: "Your site is suspended. Contact
                                     |          |                                                 | SSPL support." with the reason
  SlugConflictException              | Y        | reject, keep form values                        | Super Admin: "Slug taken"
  EdgeMappingException               | Y        | retry 3x, then mark tenant "edge not mapped",   | Super Admin alert; owner invite held
                                     |          | alert                                           | until mapping succeeds
  InviteExpiredException             | Y        | show expiry page, Super Admin can resend        | "This invite has expired. Ask SSPL to
                                     |          |                                                 | resend it."
  OtpVerificationException           | Y        | NFR-SEC-03 limits apply                          | standard OTP error
  GatewayStatusTimeoutException      | Y        | show item as "Couldn't check", never as done    | "Couldn't check Razorpay. Retry"
```
No unrescued paths remain once CEO-A2 is decided. No catch-all handlers are proposed; each log line carries tenant id, actor and action.

### Section 3: Security and threat model (new launch paths)

| Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Create pilot tenant misused by a compromised Super Admin account | Low | High | Yes: FR-SA-001 SSO + MFA + SSPL IP range; action audited (A2) |
| Owner invite link forwarded or intercepted | Med | High | Yes: single-use, 72h expiry, phone OTP on acceptance, owner sets MFA before Razorpay or domain (FR-TA-007) |
| Suspend/reinstate abused | Low | Med | Yes: FR-SA-011 reason + audit; tenant notified |
| Bot flood, spam or stored XSS through the public Book-a-demo / pilot request form | High | Med | No: FR-MW-008 has no spam protection, privacy notice, storage or retention rule (CEO-A2) |
| Owner phone and email in that form under DPDP | High | Med | No: no notice or retention (CEO-A2) |

Finding A3 `[P2] (8/10)`: FR-MW-008 is now the only public intake at launch (CEO-S1), and it collects owners' names, phones and emails with no spam protection, privacy notice, storage or retention rule. FR-TW-006 already defines spam protection for tenant contact forms. Decision row CEO-A2.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO-A2, product owner (Section 3) | FR-MW-008 (04-srs:40); FR-TW-006 (04-srs:219); NFR-SEC-05/06; CEO-S1 accepted scope | "Book a demo" form creates a lead in SSPL's CRM / emails sales; no protection, notice or retention | Same protection as FR-TW-006, privacy notice, leads stored in Super Admin, notify through the email outbox, 12-month retention | approved | D6 answer 'Harden and store (recommended)', 2026-11-03; scope as in CEO-A2 Accepted scope |

Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (harden the form) | B (as written) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Spam protection | FR-TW-006 pattern | none | honeypot + per-IP rate limit (5/hour) + invisible challenge | none |
| Where requests are stored | FR-MW-008 "SSPL's CRM" (no such system in 08-architecture) | undefined | "Pilot requests" list in Super Admin (name, phone, email, space, city, seats, aggregators used, message, status) | email to sales only |
| Notification | FR-MW-008 | email to sales | email through the existing outbox with retry; request is saved before notify | email; lost if send fails |
| Privacy | DPDP; FR-BA-003 pattern | none | notice under the form (purpose: pilot contact; retention 12 months; grievance contact) | none |
| Output safety | NFR-SEC-05/06 | n/a | stored as plain text, rendered escaped in Super Admin | n/a |
| Tests | none | none | honeypot and rate limit reject; saved when email fails; script in message renders as text | none |

## currentDecision (CEO-A2)
Question: D6 — CEO-A2: How should the public pilot request form (Book a demo) be protected and stored at launch?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review Section 3 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, FR-MW-008 after CEO-S1.
ELI10: With public sign-up moved to the Billing release, the "Book a demo" form on cowos.in is the only way an outside owner can reach SSPL. Today the requirement just says it emails sales. It has no bot protection, no privacy notice for the phone numbers and emails it collects, and if the email fails the request is lost. The same protection already exists for tenant contact forms, so we can reuse it.
Stakes if we pick wrong: bots fill the inbox, a real pilot lead disappears when an email fails, or SSPL holds owners' phone numbers without the notice Indian data law expects.
Recommendation: A because it reuses the FR-TW-006 protection already being built and saves every request before notifying (zero silent failures).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=4/10
Pros / cons:
A) Harden the form and store requests (recommended)
  ✅ Bots are stopped by the same honeypot, rate limit and challenge used on tenant sites
  ✅ Every request is saved in Super Admin first, so a failed email never loses a pilot lead
  ❌ About a day of work for a small Super Admin list and the notice copy
B) Keep FR-MW-008 as written
  ✅ No extra build; the form just sends an email to sales
  ✅ Nothing new to store, so nothing extra to delete later
  ❌ No bot protection, no privacy notice, and a failed email loses the lead silently
Net: about a day of work against losing pilot leads and collecting personal data without a notice.
Header: Demo form
A) Harden and store (recommended)
Apply FR-TW-006 protection (honeypot, 5 per IP per hour, invisible challenge); save each request to a "Pilot requests" list in Super Admin before notifying sales through the email outbox with retry; show a privacy notice (purpose, 12-month retention, grievance contact); store as plain text and render escaped. Tests: honeypot and rate limit reject, request saved when email fails, script in a message renders as text. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Keep as written
Keep FR-MW-008 as a form that emails sales, with no added protection, storage or notice. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: spam, lost leads, DPDP exposure.

State: approved
Actual answer: D6 answered 'Harden and store (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: FR-MW-008 form gets FR-TW-006 protection (honeypot, 5 per IP per hour, invisible challenge); each request is saved to a 'Pilot requests' list in Super Admin before sales is notified through the email outbox with retry; a privacy notice shows purpose, 12-month retention and grievance contact; input stored as plain text and rendered escaped. Tests: honeypot and rate limit reject, request saved when email fails, script in a message renders as text.
History: none

### Section 4: Data flow and interaction edge cases

Pilot request (CEO-A2):
```
  INPUT form ──► VALIDATE (required name/phone/email/space/city; phone +91 E.164; message ≤ 2,000)
     │              ├─ nil/empty/wrong type ─► 400, inline errors, nothing saved
     │              └─ too long ─────────────► 400 "Keep it under 2,000 characters"
     ├─► GUARD honeypot / 5 per IP per hour / invisible challenge ─► fail: 400 generic, logged with IP hash
     ├─► PERSIST PilotRequest (UTF-8; Telugu, Hindi names kept)
     │              ├─ DB down/timeout ─► 503 "Please try again", no email sent
     │              └─ same phone within 24h ─► update existing request, one notification
     └─► OUTPUT 200 "We'll contact you within 2 working days" + outbox email to sales (retry; failure leaves request visible in Super Admin)
```

Create pilot tenant (A2): INPUT Super Admin form → VALIDATE slug rules (FR-TA-005) → PERSIST Tenant(Active, pilot flag) + KVS mapping → OUTPUT Owner invite. Shadows: slug taken → 409; KVS write fails → retry then "edge not mapped" alert, invite held; double submit → idempotency key, one tenant.

Async ordering. Invariant: after every render job finishes, the tenant's live pointer shows the newest owner-chosen version (publish or rollback, FR-TA-044) in the page variant that matches the tenant's current state. Renders share one mutable value: the live pointer.

```
  Schedule 1 (suspend vs publish, covered by CEO-A1)
  t | owner publish v8          | SA suspend + RT4              | state / pointer
  1 | render v8 (state Active)  |                               | Active / v7
  2 | ...paused                 | state:=Suspended, render S    | Suspended / v7
  3 |                           | switch: state Suspended ✔     | Suspended / S
  4 | switch: state Suspended ✘ |                               | Suspended / S   ✔ invariant holds
     (reverse order: v8 switches first at t2, then S switches at t3 → Suspended / S ✔)

  Schedule 2 (data refresh vs publish, NOT covered)
  t | data-refresh of v7 (FR-PL-011 price edit, or RT4 reinstate) | owner publish v8 | state / pointer
  1 | render v7 with new prices                                    |                  | Active / v7
  2 | ...paused (low priority)                                     | render v8, switch| Active / v8
  3 | switch to re-rendered v7                                     |                  | Active / v7   ✘ v8 lost
     (reverse order: refresh switches at t2, v8 at t3 → v8 ✔; the bad order needs only a slow refresh)
```

Finding A4 `[P1] (8/10)`: data-refresh renders (FR-PL-011 automatic rebuilds and RT4 reinstate) re-render "the live version" and can finish after a newer owner publish, putting the older version back live without anyone choosing it. CEO-A1's state check does not catch this (state stays Active). FR-PL-011 already ships at launch, so the race exists with or without the CEO deferrals. Decision row CEO-A3.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO-A3, product owner (Section 4) | FR-PL-010/011 (04-srs:334-335); FR-TA-044 (04-srs:89); R11 (design:1503); CEO-A1 accepted scope | Data-refresh renders switch the live pointer unconditionally | Data-refresh renders switch only if the pointer still shows the version they started from (compare-and-set); otherwise discard | approved | D7 answer 'Compare-and-set (recommended)', 2026-11-03; scope as in CEO-A3 Accepted scope |

Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (compare-and-set) | B (one render queue per tenant) | C (do nothing) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Refresh finishing after a newer publish | FR-PL-010/011 | overwrites newer version | discarded; the newer publish already has fresh data | cannot happen: renders run one at a time per tenant | overwrites newer version |
| Owner publish and rollback | FR-TA-044 (approved) | switch unconditionally | unchanged (still blocked while Suspended, CEO-A1) | unchanged, but wait behind queued refreshes | unchanged |
| Refresh priority below publishes | R11 (approved) | yes | yes | lost: FIFO per tenant | yes |
| CEO-A1 state check | approved | at pointer switch | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
| Tests | none | none | paused refresh released after publish leaves v8 live; refresh with no newer publish still goes live | publish waits behind refresh, final pointer v8 | none |

## currentDecision (CEO-A3)
Question: D7 — CEO-A3: How should a website refresh avoid overwriting a newer publish?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review Section 4 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, FR-PL-010/011 render pipeline.
ELI10: When an owner changes a price, the site is quietly rebuilt in the background with the new price. If the owner publishes a new version of the site while that background rebuild is still running, the slow rebuild can finish second and put the old version back live, so the owner's new page disappears. The fix is for the background rebuild to check, just before it goes live, that the site it started from is still the live one, and to throw itself away if not.
Stakes if we pick wrong: an owner publishes a new page, sees it go live, and minutes later finds the old site back with no explanation.
Recommendation: A because a compare-and-set at the one place the pointer changes closes the race without slowing publishes (explicit over clever, smallest diff).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=9/10, C=3/10
Pros / cons:
A) Compare-and-set on refresh renders (recommended)
  ✅ A slow background rebuild can never replace a newer publish or rollback
  ✅ Publishes keep priority over refreshes, as R11 already approved
  ❌ One more check in the pointer switch, with a test that pauses and releases renders
B) One render queue per tenant
  ✅ Renders for a tenant never overlap, so no ordering bugs of any kind
  ✅ Simple to reason about: jobs run in the order they were queued
  ❌ An owner's publish waits behind queued price refreshes, against R11's priority rule
C) Do nothing
  ✅ No extra work in the render pipeline
  ✅ The bad order needs a slow refresh and a publish in the same minute
  ❌ When it happens, the owner's newest site silently disappears
Net: one guarded check against either slower publishes or rare silent reverts.
Header: Render race
A) Compare-and-set (recommended)
Each data-refresh render (FR-PL-011 and RT4 reinstate) records the live version it started from and switches the pointer only if it still points there, else discards itself and logs; publish and rollback switch as before (blocked while Suspended per CEO-A1). Tests: a paused refresh released after a publish leaves the new version live; a refresh with no newer publish still goes live. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Per-tenant render queue
Run all renders for one tenant one at a time in queue order; data refresh resolves the live version when it starts. Tests: publish queued behind a refresh ends with the new version live. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk; publishes can wait behind refreshes.
C) Do nothing
Keep unconditional pointer switches for refresh renders. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: rare silent reverts.

State: approved
Actual answer: D7 answered 'Compare-and-set (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Each data-refresh render (FR-PL-011 automatic rebuilds and RT4 reinstate) records the live version it started from and switches the live pointer only if it still points there; otherwise it discards itself and logs. Owner publish and rollback switch as before, blocked while Suspended (CEO-A1). Tests: a paused refresh released after a publish leaves the new version live; a refresh with no newer publish still goes live.
History: none

Interaction edge cases (new launch interactions):

| Interaction | Edge case | Handled? | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| Create pilot tenant | double submit | Yes | idempotency key, one tenant (A2) |
| Owner invite | resend while old link outstanding | Yes | resend revokes the old link (A2) |
| Owner invite | opened after 72h | Yes | expiry page, Super Admin resends |
| Suspend | double-click | Yes | second action is a no-op, already Suspended |
| Suspend | during first-publish review | Yes | review paused; reinstate resumes it (CEO-A1 blocks publish while Suspended) |
| Suspend / reinstate | publish or price refresh in flight | Yes | CEO-A1 state check + CEO-A3 compare-and-set |
| Setup checklist | Razorpay disconnected after "done" | Yes | item re-checked on load, flips back to not done; timeout shows "Couldn't check" |
| Pilot request form | double submit / same phone within 24h | Yes | dedupe, one notification (CEO-A2) |
| Theme 3-4 release | pilot switches theme | Yes | normal publish path; RT7 loads the new shell chunk |

No unhandled edge cases remain.

### Section 5: Code quality

- Finding Q1 `[P3] (7/10)`, corrected without a question: CEO-S1's scope said "manual Super Admin state change", which could be read as any state. The launch actions are exactly FR-SA-011 suspend/unpublish and reinstate, plus Create pilot tenant (A2); Restricted, PastDue and Trial have no launch action and the API returns 409 (Section 1 state diagram).
- RT4 now has two trigger sources (FR-SA-011 at launch, dunning at the Billing release). Both should raise one TenantStateChanged event that RT4 consumes, so the Billing release adds a producer, not a second caller path.
- No duplicated behavior: the pilot request form reuses FR-TW-006 protection; the owner invite reuses FR-TA-172; the checklist is FR-TA-002 and 11A's handoff screen.
- No over-engineering found; no method in the new paths needs more than 5 branches.

### Section 6: Tests

```
  NEW AT LAUNCH (CEO)                     TYPE         HAPPY                         FAILURE / EDGE
  Create pilot tenant + Owner invite      Integration  tenant Active+pilot, invite   slug 409; KVS fail holds invite; expired
                                                       accepted, MFA set             link 410; double submit → one tenant
  RT4 on FR-SA-011 suspend/reinstate      Integration  10A page live ≤ ~2 min;       render fails 3x → alert; owner publish 409
                                                       reinstate restores site       while Suspended
  Pointer switch state check (CEO-A1)     Integration  -                             paused publish released after suspend →
                                          (pause/release)                            suspended page stays live
  Refresh compare-and-set (CEO-A3)        Integration  refresh with no newer publish paused refresh released after publish →
                                          (pause/release) goes live                  new version stays live
  Pilot request form (CEO-A2)             Integration  saved + outbox email          honeypot / 6th request per hour / challenge
                                          + E2E                                      reject; saved when email fails; script as text
  Setup checklist (FR-TA-002)             Unit + E2E   all done → publish allowed    Razorpay timeout → "Couldn't check"
  No public sign-up at launch             E2E          -                             /signup and sign-up API return 404 at launch
  Two launch themes                       CI           Lighthouse + axe pass on both -
  Pilot scorecard (CEO-A4, if accepted)   Integration  counts match fixture bookings staff-created and offline bookings excluded
```
Assertion checks (each `no_claim` is what a green result does not prove):
- Suspend: protects=a suspended tenant's site shows the 10A page; fails_when=pointer switch skips the state re-read; no_claim=CDN propagation time beyond staging.
- Refresh race: protects=the newest owner-chosen version stays live; fails_when=refresh switches without comparing the starting version; no_claim=none beyond protects.
- Pilot request: protects=every accepted request is saved before notify; fails_when=notify runs before persist or failure drops the row; no_claim=real email delivery (mocked SES).
- Invite: protects=only the invited person can claim the Owner seat; fails_when=link reusable or not expired; no_claim=email interception outside Cowos.
2am-Friday test: create a pilot, accept the invite, publish, suspend during a price refresh, reinstate, publish again; the final live site is the last publish. Hostile QA: replay an accepted invite link; submit the demo form 50 times from one IP. Chaos: kill the worker mid-render during suspend; on restart the job retries and the state check still holds. Pyramid stays unit-heavy; pause/release tests use controlled hooks, not sleeps, to avoid flakiness.

### Section 7: Performance

No issues found. The new paths are low volume (20 pilots, a handful of demo requests a day). The scorecard query (if CEO-A4 is accepted) groups bookings by tenant and month and needs an index on (tenant_id, confirmed_at) filtered to paid online bookings, built monthly or on demand. The demo form's rate limit reuses the FR-TW-006 limiter. Suspend renders are one page per theme and run at RT4's low priority.

### Section 8: Observability

- Logs: every new path logs tenant id, actor, action and outcome; render aborts (CEO-A1, CEO-A3) log at info with versions.
- Alerts: suspend render failed 3x; KVS mapping failed on pilot creation; pilot request notification failed; existing fair-use cap alerts (Pilot Rules #5).
- Runbooks: "Suspend a tenant" (reason, check 10A page live, notify), "Create and hand over a pilot" (Pilot Rules #9 steps, invite resend).
- People single point of failure: until the Billing release, the named onboarding owner (Pilot Rules #9) is the only way a tenant gets created. The runbook above lets a second SSPL person do it.

Finding O1 `[P1] (8/10)`: the success criteria and expansion gate (design:36, 105-110; G2 01-vision:27) are measured in paid online bookings per pilot per month, but the only planned business dashboard, FR-SA-051, shows sign-ups, trials, conversions, MRR and churn, which stay empty until the Billing release (CEO-S1). FR-SA-002 shows bookings in the last 30 days, not paid online bookings by month. Nothing shows whether the pilot is working. Decision row CEO-A4.

## currentDecision (CEO-A4)
Question: D8 — CEO-A4: Should Super Admin show a pilot scorecard at launch instead of the sign-up and revenue dashboard?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review Section 8 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, success criteria, expansion gate and FR-SA-051.
ELI10: The pilot is judged by one number: how many paid online bookings each pilot space gets per month (the target is 10 or more, for 60% of pilots, and the second-city gate needs 10 pilots at 10+ for 3 months running). The only dashboard in the plan shows sign-ups, trials and revenue, which are all empty until billing ships. A small scorecard showing each pilot's monthly paid online bookings, and whether the gate is met, tells SSPL every month whether the product is working.
Stakes if we pick wrong: SSPL reaches month 6 of the pilot without knowing which spaces are getting real bookings, or counts staff-entered bookings and declares success that isn't real.
Recommendation: A because the success criteria are only useful if someone can see them; this measures them directly and moves the empty billing metrics to when they have data.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=4/10
Pros / cons:
A) Pilot scorecard at launch (recommended)
  ✅ Shows each pilot's paid online bookings per month and whether the 3-month gate is met
  ✅ Counts only real customer bookings, so staff-entered or offline bookings can't inflate it
  ❌ About a day of backend and frontend work for one Super Admin page and its CSV
B) Keep FR-SA-051 as written
  ✅ No change to the planned Super Admin dashboard
  ✅ Booking exports already exist, so someone can count by hand in a spreadsheet
  ❌ The dashboard is empty until billing ships, and the success numbers need manual counting
Net: a day of work against judging the pilot from hand-counted spreadsheets.
Header: Scorecard
A) Pilot scorecard (recommended)
Add a Super Admin pilot scorecard: per pilot per month, paid online bookings (customer-made, captured, not fully refunded; staff-created and offline excluded), cancellations, first publish, free-period end, notification and OTP cap usage; roll-up of pilots live, pilots at ≥10 this month, pilots with 3 consecutive months at ≥10 and gate met yes/no; CSV export. FR-SA-051's sign-up, trial and revenue metrics move to the Billing release. Tests: fixture month counts, exclusions, streak across month boundaries. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Keep as written
Keep FR-SA-051 (sign-ups, trials, conversions, MRR, churn) at launch and FR-SA-002's 30-day booking count; pilot success is counted from exports. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: no visible success measure.

State: approved
Actual answer: D8 answered 'Pilot scorecard (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Super Admin pilot scorecard at launch: per pilot per month, paid online bookings (customer-made in the booking app, payment captured, not fully refunded; staff-created and offline excluded), cancellations, first publish date, free-period end date, notification and OTP cap usage; roll-up of pilots live, pilots at >=10 this month, pilots with 3 consecutive months at >=10, and expansion gate met yes/no; CSV export. FR-SA-051 sign-up, trial, conversion, MRR and churn metrics move to the Billing release. Tests: fixture month counts, exclusions, 3-month streak across month boundaries.

### Section 9: Deployment and rollout

- Launch carries no billing schema. The Billing release adds BillingProfile, CostPool, TenantCostShare, HostingInvoice and AdjustmentLine as additive migrations and backfills a BillingProfile for every existing tenant (pilots with their free-period end date, SSPL test and demo tenants). Expand-only, zero downtime.
- Flags at launch: public sign-up and platform billing are not deployed; `/signup` and the sign-up API return 404 (Section 6 test). The pilot request form (CEO-A2) is the only intake.
- Billing release order (from Pilot Rules #7): deploy billing dark → bill SSPL's paid test tenant → open public sign-up and the wizard (CEO-S2) → run one dunning cycle end to end before the first pilot converts. The first billing run covers the first full calendar month after the release; no invoices are issued for earlier months (pilots are free and no other tenants exist).
- Themes 3-4 (CEO-S3): a theme package deploy with no migration; the catalog entry stays hidden until its Lighthouse, axe and screen-reader checks pass.
- Post-deploy checks at launch (first hour): create a pilot on production, accept its invite, suspend and reinstate an SSPL demo tenant and confirm the 10A page, submit the demo form and see it in Super Admin, open the pilot scorecard.
- Rollback: launch-only additions are a Super Admin action, a form, a dashboard and the RT4 trigger. If RT4 misbehaves, Super Admin falls back to FR-SA-011 unpublish while it is fixed; the render guards (CEO-A1, CEO-A3) only ever discard a render, so turning them off is never needed.

### Section 10: Long-term trajectory

- Debt: billing is built about 4 months after launch against live data (backfill above); documentation debt is the pack, which still describes flat plans (task T9/DT8) and now also these CEO deferrals.
- Path dependency: none new. The deferred billing design is unchanged and stays recorded in the eng review.
- Reversibility: 4/5. Each deferral can be pulled forward; the launch schema is additive.
- Knowledge: the Billing release depends on this document's eng review sections; TODOS.md entries point to them.
- 1-year question: a new engineer can follow it, provided the pack is updated (T9).

Finding T1 `[P1] (9/10)`: goal G3 in the pack is "150 paying tenants; gross margin ≥70% after AWS and messaging" (01-vision:28). Premise 5 (owner decision, 2026-11-02) sets SSPL's charge to the real AWS cost plus messaging at cost, so gross margin is 0% by design and G3 can never be met. The plan therefore has no SSPL-level success measure at all; the remaining criteria (design:105-110) measure pilots, not SSPL. Decision row CEO-A5.

## currentDecision (CEO-A5)
Question: D9 — CEO-A5: What should replace the pack's 70% gross-margin goal, which "hosting cost only" makes impossible?
Project/branch/task: main, CEO review Section 10 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, goal G3 vs Premise 5.
ELI10: The pack's business goal says SSPL should earn a 70% margin. You decided SSPL charges tenants only the real AWS cost, so the margin is zero by design and that goal can never be hit. Right now the plan has no goal at all for SSPL itself, only for the pilots. A cost-recovery goal asks a question the plan can answer: do paying tenants cover the platform's own running cost by month 18, and how much does SSPL still carry?
Stakes if we pick wrong: SSPL funds the platform with no number that says whether it is on track, and the pack keeps a target everyone knows is false.
Recommendation: A because it is the only financial measure that fits Premise 5 and can be read straight from the billing data the plan already builds.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Cost-recovery goal (recommended)
  ✅ Measurable every month from CostPool once billing runs, with no new data source
  ✅ Shows plainly what SSPL still pays for (staging, dev, team, pilot year)
  ❌ Makes SSPL's ongoing subsidy visible on a dashboard, which may raise questions internally
B) Keep only 150 paying tenants
  ✅ Simplest wording; one number everyone already knows from the pack
  ✅ No new tracking beyond counting paying tenants
  ❌ Says nothing about whether tenant payments cover even the platform's running cost
C) Leave G3 open for now
  ✅ Leaves room to define SSPL's real return (for example a later paid product) with leadership
  ✅ No commitment made in a technical review about a business goal
  ❌ The pack keeps a goal that cannot be met until someone revisits it
Net: a measurable cost-recovery target vs a simple count vs deciding the business goal elsewhere.
Header: SSPL goal
A) Cost-recovery goal (recommended)
Replace G3 with: 150 paying tenants, and by launch + 18 months tenant hosting payments cover 100% of the production AWS bill and messaging cost, tracked monthly from CostPool after the Billing release, with SSPL's unrecovered cost (staging, dev, pilot year, team) shown alongside. Update 01-vision and 00-start-here with the pack update (T9). Effort S (spec edits human: ~1h / CC: ~10min; tracking rides on the Billing release). Low risk.
B) Tenant count only
Replace G3 with 150 paying tenants and drop the margin target. Effort S (spec edit human: ~15min / CC: ~5min). Low risk.
C) Leave G3 open
Mark G3 as contradicted by Premise 5 and add an open question in 13-risks for leadership to define SSPL's return. Effort S (spec edit human: ~15min / CC: ~5min). Medium risk: no SSPL measure meanwhile.

State: approved
Actual answer: D9 answered 'Cost-recovery goal (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Replace G3 with: 150 paying tenants, and by launch + 18 months tenant hosting payments cover 100% of the production AWS bill and messaging cost, tracked monthly from CostPool after the Billing release, with SSPL's unrecovered cost (staging, dev, pilot year, team) shown alongside. Update 01-vision and 00-start-here with the pack update (T9).

## Re-review 2 decision ledger

### R17: how live-pointer changes reach the CloudFront KeyValueStore
Finding: R17, P1, confidence 8/10, docs/requirements/08-architecture.md:180 ("The edge routing table (host → tenant, version, state) lives in CloudFront KeyValueStore, updated by the Domains/Sites modules on publish, rollback, domain change, or state change") with CEO-A1/CEO-A3 accepted scope (design: CEO-A1, CEO-A3); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 2
Plan baseline: each publish, rollback, domain or state job writes its own KVS value after its DB switch (08 §6); CEO-A1 and CEO-A3 guard only the DB pointer
Runtime evidence: no code. AWS UpdateKeys requires IfMatch with the whole store's current ETag (AWS API reference); concurrent writers get 409 and retry, so the KVS write order can differ from the DB commit order. Example: publish v8 commits, then suspend commits; the suspend KVS write lands first; the publish job retries and writes v8 last, so the edge serves v8 while the DB says Suspended.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R17 who writes KVS | every job writes its own value | one EdgeSyncWorker; jobs only mark the tenant dirty (outbox row) in the same DB transaction as their switch | every job, after reading the current key and skipping if its pointer sequence is older | every job writes its own value |
| R17 value written | the job's own payload | the tenant's current DB pointer and state, read at send time | the job's payload, plus a monotonic pointer sequence | the job's payload |
| R17 conflict handling | retry, may land out of order | 409 → re-describe ETag, retry; batches many dirty tenants per UpdateKeys | 409 → re-read key and ETag, compare sequence, retry | retry, may land out of order |
| R17 rollback speed (FR-TA-044, 60 s) | seconds | seconds (worker polls the outbox continuously) | seconds | seconds |
| CEO-A1, CEO-A3 DB guards | approved | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
| R17 tests | none | publish and suspend committed in one order, KVS writes forced in the reverse order → KVS ends equal to DB; worker restart mid-batch → resends current DB value | same reversed-order test via sequence check | none |
Question D2:
D2 — R17: How should pointer changes reach the CloudFront edge without landing out of order?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review 2 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, CEO-A1/CEO-A3 guards and 08-architecture §6.
ELI10: The database now decides correctly which version of a site is live, even when a publish and a suspension happen at the same moment. But visitors are served by CloudFront, which reads a separate lookup table. Each job copies its own answer into that table, and AWS makes concurrent writers retry, so an older answer can arrive last and win. Then the database says "suspended" while visitors still see the normal site.
Stakes if we pick wrong: a suspended space's site, or an old version, stays live at the edge with nothing in the database showing anything wrong.
Recommendation: A because one writer that always copies the database's current value cannot write a stale answer, and it is the boring outbox pattern (explicit over clever).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=8/10, C=3/10
Pros / cons:
A) One edge-sync writer (recommended)
  ✅ The edge always ends up matching the database, whatever order jobs finish in
  ✅ Batches many tenants into one KVS update, so busy publish days cause fewer 409 retries
  ❌ A new worker and outbox table to build, deploy and monitor before launch
B) Sequence check in every job
  ✅ No new worker; each job still pushes its own change straight to the edge
  ✅ Older writes are skipped by comparing a pointer sequence before writing
  ❌ Every job repeats read-compare-write logic, and a crashed job leaves the edge behind
C) Do nothing
  ✅ No extra work beyond what 08 §6 already plans
  ✅ Most of the time jobs don't overlap and the order is right
  ❌ When they do overlap, the edge silently serves the wrong site
Net: one small worker vs repeated per-job checks vs a silent edge mismatch.
Header: Edge sync
Options:
A) One edge-sync writer (recommended)
Pointer and state changes add a tenant row to an edge_sync outbox in the same transaction as the DB switch; one EdgeSyncWorker drains it, reads each tenant's current DB pointer and state at send time, and writes them to KVS with IfMatch, retrying on 409 and batching tenants. Tests: reversed KVS write order ends with KVS equal to DB; worker restart resends current values. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) Sequence check in every job
Each job stores a monotonic pointer sequence with its KVS value and, before writing, reads the current key and skips if its sequence is older, retrying on 409. Tests: reversed write order ends with the newer value. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Medium risk: logic repeated in every writer; crash leaves edge stale.
C) Do nothing
Keep per-job KVS writes as in 08 §6. Effort S (zero implementation change). High risk of silent edge mismatch under concurrency.

State: approved
Actual answer: D2 answered 'One edge-sync writer (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Pointer and state changes add a tenant row to an edge_sync outbox in the same transaction as the DB switch; one EdgeSyncWorker drains it, reads each tenant's current DB pointer and state at send time, and writes them to KVS with IfMatch, retrying on 409 and batching tenants. Publish, rollback, domain, state and pilot-creation jobs no longer write KVS directly. Tests: reversed KVS write order ends with KVS equal to DB; worker restart resends current values.
History: none

### Re-review 2 Section 1: Architecture

```
  Owner publish / rollback ─┐                         ┌─ CEO-A1 state check
  Data refresh (PL-011, RT4)┼─► DB: tenant pointer ───┤  CEO-A3 compare-and-set (R18)
  SA suspend / reinstate ───┘   + edge_sync outbox row └─ same transaction
                                       │
                                       ▼
                    EdgeSyncWorker (single consumer, R17)
                    read CURRENT DB pointer+state ──► KVS UpdateKeys(IfMatch)
                         409 → re-describe ETag, retry; batch tenants
                                       │
                                       ▼
                    CloudFront Function reads KVS ──► S3 prefix / 10A page
```

Findings (most severe first):
- `[P1] (confidence: 8/10) docs/requirements/08-architecture.md:180` — "updated by the Domains/Sites modules on publish, rollback, domain change, or state change": per-job KVS writes can land out of DB order (R17). Disposition: accepted, D2 (one edge-sync writer).
- `[P2] (confidence: 7/10) design: CEO-A2 accepted scope` — "save each request to a 'Pilot requests' list in Super Admin": pilot requests are SSPL data, not tenant data, so the table sits in the platform schema outside tenant RLS, readable only by the Super Admin role (NFR-SEC-01). Disposition: factual placement following NFR-SEC-01 and eng D4; no new behavior, no question.
- `[P3] (confidence: 7/10) design: CEO-A2 accepted scope` — the demo form runs on cowos.in, which is static; its POST goes to the API like FR-TW-006 tenant forms, so no new surface beyond one public endpoint. Disposition: noted, no question.

Production failure per new path: EdgeSyncWorker down → outbox grows, edge stays on the last value; alert on outbox age > 2 min (Section 4 of this re-review). Create pilot → KVS mapping now also goes through the outbox, so "edge not mapped" is the outbox-age alert. Pilot request email fails → request already saved (CEO-A2).

### R18: ordering between two data-refresh renders of the same version
Finding: R18, P2, confidence 7/10, design CEO-A3 accepted scope ("records the live version it started from and switches the live pointer only if it still points there"); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 2
Plan baseline: CEO-A3 (D7, approved 2026-11-03): refresh renders switch only if the pointer still shows the version they started from
Runtime evidence: no code. Two price edits a minute apart start two refreshes of v7. If the version id alone is compared, the older refresh can finish last and put older prices live. If the full pointer (version + render) is compared, the newer refresh is discarded whenever the older one finishes first, so stale prices stay until the next edit. Neither form matches the intent "newest data stays live".
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R18 refresh ordering rule | version id compared only (CEO-A3) | tenant.data_seq increments in the same transaction as every website-visible data change; each render records the data_seq it read; pointer stores (version_id, data_seq); a refresh switches only if version_id is unchanged and its data_seq is higher | at most one refresh per tenant runs at a time; a request during a run sets a rerun flag; the refresh reads data at start and runs again if flagged | as CEO-A3 |
| Refresh vs newer publish (CEO-A3) | discarded | discarded (version_id changed) | discarded | discarded |
| Publish priority over refresh (R11) | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Edge write path (R17) | one EdgeSyncWorker (D2) | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
| Customer price at payment | 2A re-quote is authoritative | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged |
| R18 tests | none | two refreshes released in both orders → newer data_seq live; refresh after publish discarded | two requests during a run → one rerun, newest data live | none |
Question D3:
D3 — R18: When two background website refreshes overlap, how do we make sure the newest prices win?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review 2 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, CEO-A3 refresh compare-and-set.
ELI10: When an owner edits two prices a minute apart, two background rebuilds of the same site version start. The rule we approved checks only "is the same version still live?", which is true for both, so the older rebuild can finish last and show the older prices. A simple counter that goes up with every price or name change lets each rebuild know whether it is newer than what's live.
Stakes if we pick wrong: the website shows a price the owner already changed until their next edit; customers still pay the right price because checkout re-quotes (2A), but they see a mismatch.
Recommendation: A because a data counter makes "newest data wins" explicit in the one place the pointer changes and keeps refreshes running in parallel (explicit over clever).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=9/10, C=5/10
Pros / cons:
A) Data counter on each render (recommended)
  ✅ Whichever refresh finishes last, the newest prices end up live
  ✅ Keeps the approved refresh-vs-publish rule; only adds one number to the pointer
  ❌ Every website-visible data change must bump the counter, or the check sees stale data as new
B) One refresh at a time per tenant
  ✅ No ordering bugs between refreshes, because they never overlap for a tenant
  ✅ Bursts of edits collapse into at most one extra run
  ❌ A long refresh delays the next price change reaching the site by a full render
C) Keep CEO-A3 as written
  ✅ No extra work beyond the approved compare-and-set
  ✅ Checkout still charges the right price because of the 2A re-quote
  ❌ The site can show an older price after two quick edits until the next change
Net: a counter that guarantees freshness vs serialized refreshes vs an occasional stale price.
Header: Refresh order
Options:
A) Data counter on each render (recommended)
Add tenant.data_seq, incremented in the same transaction as every website-visible data change (inventory names, prices, hours); every render records the data_seq it read; the pointer stores (version_id, data_seq); a data refresh switches only if version_id is unchanged and its data_seq is higher, else discards and logs. Tests: two refreshes released in both orders leave the newest data live; a refresh after a publish is discarded. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) One refresh at a time per tenant
Run at most one data refresh per tenant; a request during a run sets a rerun flag and the refresh runs again after finishing; the CEO-A3 version check stays. Tests: two requests during a run produce one rerun with the newest data. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk; slower propagation under bursts.
C) Keep CEO-A3 as written
Compare the version id only, as approved. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: stale prices after quick edits.

State: approved
Actual answer: D3 answered 'Data counter on each render (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Add tenant.data_seq, incremented in the same transaction as every website-visible data change (inventory names, prices, hours); every render records the data_seq it read; the pointer stores (version_id, data_seq); a data refresh switches only if version_id is unchanged and its data_seq is higher, else discards and logs. Refines CEO-A3. Tests: two refreshes released in both orders leave the newest data live; a refresh after a publish is discarded.
History: clarified 2026-11-03 during the pack v0.2 update: tenant state changes (suspend, reinstate, Restricted) also bump data_seq, because the state changes what the site shows; without it the CEO-A1 reinstate render would be discarded and its approved test (reinstate restores the site) could not pass

### Re-review 2 Section 2: Code quality

Findings so far:
- `[P1] (confidence: 8/10) docs/requirements/02-personas-roles-permissions.md:56` — "only with an active, tenant-approved support grant (time-limited, audited)", with Pilot Rules #9 (design:71) "SSPL does setup through a tenant-approved support grant" and 11A (design:1172) "first-login handoff screen: Review what we set up". A pilot created by Super Admin (CEO A2) has no Owner until the invite is accepted, so nobody can approve the grant SSPL needs to set the space up before the owner's first login. Decision row R19.

### R19: how SSPL sets up a new pilot before its owner can approve a support grant
Finding: R19, P1, confidence 8/10, 02-personas:56 with design:71 and design:1172; reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 2
Plan baseline: Pilot Rules #9 (approved 2026-11-02) setup through a tenant-approved support grant; 11A (approved) handoff screen "Review what we set up"; CEO A2 (no question; follows CEO-S1) create pilot then Owner invite; D1 of this review: Owner invite reuses FR-TA-172
Runtime evidence: no code. FR-SA-030 grants are read-only by default and tenant-approved; a tenant with no Owner has no approver.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R19 setup access before handover | undefined (no approver) | pre-handover setup grant: write access created with the pilot, valid only while the tenant has no Owner, unpublished and has no live payments (Pilot Rules #1); ends automatically when the Owner accepts the invite | Owner accepts the invite first, then approves a time-limited write support grant from the handoff screen; SSPL sets up afterwards |
| R19 what the owner sees | 11A "Review what we set up" | 11A handoff lists every change SSPL made, from the audit log | 11A shows an empty tenant and a "Let SSPL set up your space" grant request |
| After handover | tenant-approved grants (FR-SA-030) | unchanged | unchanged |
| Customer data exposure | none before go-live | none: grant ends before publish or payments are possible | none |
| Audit | FR-SA-030 full audit | every pre-handover write audited, shown at handover | FR-SA-030 full audit |
| R19 tests | none | grant writes allowed with no Owner; rejected once Owner exists or tenant publishes; handoff lists the audited changes | write rejected without approved grant; approved grant allows writes; expires on time |
Question D4:
D4 — R19: How should SSPL set up a new pilot before its owner has logged in to approve access?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review 2 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, Pilot Rules #9, 11A and the Create pilot action.
ELI10: The rules say SSPL may only change a space's settings with a support grant that the space's owner approves. But now SSPL creates the pilot space itself and the owner only logs in later, so for the first days there is no owner to approve anything, and SSPL can't do the setup the plan promises. Either SSPL gets a setup pass that only works until the owner takes over, or the owner logs in first and approves access before SSPL starts.
Stakes if we pick wrong: pilot onboarding stalls waiting for owners to log in, or SSPL gets write access with no record the owner can check.
Recommendation: A because it matches the approved handoff flow ("Review what we set up"), ends on its own before any customer data or payments can exist, and every change is shown to the owner at handover.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Setup pass until the owner takes over (recommended)
  ✅ SSPL can prepare the whole space before the owner's first login, as 11A and Pilot Rules #9 describe
  ✅ The pass ends by itself when the owner accepts the invite, before publishing or payments are possible
  ❌ A second kind of grant that skips owner approval, so its limits need their own tests
B) Owner logs in first and approves
  ✅ Keeps one rule everywhere: SSPL never writes without an owner's approval
  ✅ No special grant type to build or test
  ❌ Setup waits for each owner to log in and approve, and 11A's "Review what we set up" needs rewording
Net: faster pilot setup with a self-ending pass vs one strict approval rule with slower onboarding.
Header: Setup access
Options:
A) Setup pass until handover (recommended)
Create pilot also creates a pre-handover setup grant with write access, valid only while the tenant has no Owner, is unpublished and has no live payments; it ends automatically when the Owner accepts the invite; every write is audited and the 11A handoff lists those changes. Tests: writes allowed with no Owner; rejected once an Owner exists or the tenant publishes; handoff lists audited changes. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Owner approves first
The Owner accepts the invite, then approves a time-limited write support grant (FR-SA-030) from the handoff screen; SSPL sets up after that; 11A handoff copy changes to a grant request. Tests: writes rejected without an approved grant; approved grant allows writes and expires on time. Effort S (human: ~2h / CC: ~15min). Low risk; slower onboarding.

State: approved
Actual answer: D4 answered 'Setup pass until handover (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Create pilot also creates a pre-handover setup grant with write access, valid only while the tenant has no Owner, is unpublished and has no live payments; it ends automatically when the Owner accepts the invite; every write is audited and the 11A handoff lists those changes. Pilot Rules #9 wording updated: tenant-approved grants apply after handover. Tests: writes allowed with no Owner; rejected once an Owner exists or the tenant publishes; handoff lists audited changes.

- `[P2] (confidence: 8/10) design: CEO-A4 accepted scope` — "per pilot per month, paid online bookings (customer-made in the booking app, payment captured, not fully refunded ...)": the month a booking counts in is undefined (payment date or usage date, and in which time zone), and a full refund can land in a later month. The expansion gate (3 consecutive months at 10+) depends on it. Decision row R20.

### R20: which month a paid online booking counts in on the pilot scorecard
Finding: R20, P2, confidence 8/10, design CEO-A4 accepted scope with expansion gate (design:36); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 2
Plan baseline: CEO-A4 (D8, approved 2026-11-03) pilot scorecard, month attribution unspecified
Runtime evidence: no code. Bookings carry a payment capture time and a start time; servers run in UTC (ap-south-1), so a UTC month boundary shifts bookings made between 00:00 and 05:30 IST on the 1st.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R20 month rule | unspecified | month of payment capture in IST (Asia/Kolkata) | month the booking starts, in IST |
| R20 later full refund | unspecified | removes the booking from its capture month; past months recompute; scorecard shows "as of <time>" | removes it from its start month; past months recompute |
| Bookings for a future month | n/a | count when paid | count only once that month arrives |
| Exclusions (staff-created, offline, fully refunded) | CEO-A4 approved | unchanged | unchanged |
| R20 tests | none | capture at 23:59 and 00:01 IST on the month boundary; refund in the next month removes the earlier count | start date on the boundary; prepaid booking for next month counted next month |
Question D5:
D5 — R20: Which month should a paid online booking count in on the pilot scorecard?
Project/branch/task: main, eng re-review 2 of docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, CEO-A4 pilot scorecard and the expansion gate.
ELI10: The scorecard counts each pilot's paid online bookings per month, and the second-city decision needs 10 pilots at 10+ for three months in a row. A booking has two dates: when the customer paid and when they use the desk. Servers also keep time in UTC, so a booking paid just after midnight in India on the 1st could slip into the previous month. We need one rule so the counts can be trusted.
Stakes if we pick wrong: a pilot misses or meets the 10-booking bar because of a date rule, and the second-city decision is made on the wrong numbers.
Recommendation: A because the goal is "bookings taken per month", which is the payment month, and it is known the moment the customer pays; IST matches how owners read their month.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Payment month, India time (recommended)
  ✅ Counts a booking the moment the customer pays, so the current month is always up to date
  ✅ Uses India time, so a booking at 00:30 on the 1st lands in the month the owner expects
  ❌ A desk booked in March for April use counts in March, not in the month it is used
B) Usage month, India time
  ✅ Matches when the space is actually used, which is how occupancy is usually read
  ✅ Prepaid bookings for later months count where the customer turns up
  ❌ Advance bookings don't show until their month arrives, so the current month looks low
Net: count when money comes in vs count when the desk is used.
Header: Score month
Options:
A) Payment month, IST (recommended)
Count each paid online booking in the month its payment was captured, in Asia/Kolkata time; a later full refund removes it from that month and past months recompute, with the scorecard showing "as of <time>". Tests: captures at 23:59 and 00:01 IST on a month boundary; a next-month refund removes the earlier count. Effort S (human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Low risk.
B) Usage month, IST
Count each paid online booking in the month the booking starts, in Asia/Kolkata time; a later full refund removes it from that month and past months recompute. Tests: start dates on the boundary; a prepaid booking for next month counts next month. Effort S (human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Low risk.

State: approved
Actual answer: D5 answered 'Payment month, IST (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-03
Accepted scope: Each paid online booking counts in the month its payment was captured, in Asia/Kolkata time; a later full refund removes it from that month and past months recompute; the scorecard shows 'as of <time>'. Tests: captures at 23:59 and 00:01 IST on a month boundary; a next-month refund removes the earlier count.

# CEO Review 2 (plan-ceo-review, 2026-11-04, after the pricing revision)

Target: the 2026-11-04 revision (Revision note, Pricing and Billing Rules #1-#11) and pack v0.3. Mode: SCOPE REDUCTION (D1, answered by the product owner 2026-11-04). Each deferral is asked separately; the revision stays unless an item is deferred.

Step 0 notes: landscape (Stripe, Dynamic Business, Rework guides on usage billing; Andhra Pradesh telescopic-billing coverage) says metered billing needs usage visibility, alerts and caps to avoid bill shock, and that units should be something customers can forecast. Request units and GB-months are not. Considered for deferral and kept: onboarding wizard (self-serve is the only path in), storage meter (owner's explicit choice), daily estimate (main bill-shock protection), slab-priced wallet (owner's ask). ### C2-S1: defer the SSPL cost view
Finding: the cost view compares SSPL's actual AWS and messaging cost with billed revenue. Before launch there is no revenue to compare, and the first slab prices are set before any data exists (OQ21). The AWS Billing console and the providers' dashboards show the raw costs meanwhile.
Commitment comparison:

|---|---|---|---|---|
| CUR import + PlatformCostMonth (FR-PL-069) | Rule #10 (approved 2026-11-04) | launch | launch + 2 months | launch |
| Super Admin cost view (FR-SA-022) | Rule #10 | launch | launch + 2 months | launch |
| G3 cost-recovery measure | 01 G3 | from launch | first reading for month 1 and 2 after the view ships (data is backfilled from CUR) | from launch |
| TODO "review slab prices after 3 months" | TODOS.md | needs the view | view ships before month 3 | needs the view |
| Build time freed before launch | estimate | 0 | about 2-3 days of 1 BE + 1 FE [estimated] | 0 |

## currentDecision (C2-S1)
Question: D2 — C2-S1: Should SSPL's internal cost view ship 2 months after launch instead of at launch?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, CEO review 2 (SCOPE REDUCTION) of the pricing revision, Pricing Rule #10.
ELI10: The cost view is a Super Admin screen that puts SSPL's real AWS and SMS bills next to what tenants paid, so you can tell whether your slab prices cover the server. At launch there is nothing to compare yet, and you'll set the first prices before any data exists. AWS and the SMS providers already show the raw bills in their own dashboards. Shipping it 2 months later frees a few days before launch, and it can load the first two months from AWS's data when it arrives.
Stakes if we pick wrong: defer and the first two months' cost-vs-revenue picture is checked by hand; keep and a few launch days go to a screen with no data.
Recommendation: A because the view has nothing to show until a month of billing exists, and its data can be backfilled when it ships.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Defer to launch + 2 months (recommended)
  ✅ Frees about 2-3 days of backend and frontend work before the launch gate [estimated]
  ✅ The first two months are backfilled from AWS cost data when the view ships
  ❌ For the first two months SSPL compares cost and revenue by hand from AWS and SMS dashboards
B) Keep at launch
  ✅ The cost-recovery number (G3) is visible from the very first month
  ✅ No second release to plan for one screen and one import job
  ❌ A few launch-critical days go to a screen that has no data until the first month closes
Net: a few launch days vs having the cost-recovery screen from month one.
Header: Cost view
A) Defer to launch + 2 months (recommended)
Ship the CUR import (FR-PL-069) and the Super Admin cost view (FR-SA-022) about 2 months after launch, backfilling the first months from AWS Data Exports; until then SSPL reads costs from the AWS Billing console and provider dashboards. Effort S (zero implementation work now; spec edits human: ~30min / CC: ~5min). Low risk. ✅ Frees ~2-3 days before launch ✅ Months backfilled ❌ Manual comparison for 2 months
B) Keep at launch
Keep FR-PL-069 and FR-SA-022 in Phase 1 as written in pack v0.3. Effort S (zero implementation change). Low risk. ✅ G3 visible from month one ✅ No extra release ❌ Days spent on a screen with no data yet
