## CEO-2 review sections

Current scope: SCOPE REDUCTION (D1). C2-S1 cost-view deferral declined (D2, kept at launch). No other scope change; the 2026-11-04 revision stands as written.

### Section 1: Architecture

```
  Tenant site / booking app (CloudFront + S3) ──► CloudFront standard logs ──► S3 ──► daily log metering job ──┐
  /api/* ──► one app server (API, admin, booking, worker, renderer) ──► API request counter (batched) ─────────┤
  S3 media ──► daily storage snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
                                                                                                               ▼
  Super Admin rate card (versions, slabs) ───────────────────────────────────────────► UsageDaily per tenant/meter/IST day
                                                                                                               │
                         ┌───────────────────── daily estimate (billing page) ◄────────────────────────────────┤
                         └───────────────────── rating job on the 1st ──► ServerInvoice + lines ──► Razorpay link / UPI Autopay
  Notification send ──► MonthlyMeterCount + slab price + wallet debit (one atomic step, R4) ──► provider
  Managed PostgreSQL (RDS): all of the above; CUR 2.0 ──► PlatformCostMonth ──► SSPL cost view
```

Single points of failure: the app server (admin, booking, API, worker, rating) and the log-metering job (no logs, no request units). RDS, S3 and CloudFront are managed.

Finding A1 `[P1] (8/10)`: the request meter counts every CloudFront request on the tenant's hosts (Rule #3, design:66; FR-PL-066). One page view fetches the HTML plus its images, CSS, JS and fonts, typically dozens of files, so "1,000 requests" can be a few dozen visits, and the count moves with theme and photo choices the owner can't see. The owner's answer on 2026-11-04 picked the option described as "requests served for the tenant's website and booking app (page views, bookings, API calls)". Decision row C2-A1.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2-A1, product owner (Section 1) | Rule #3 (design:66); FR-PL-066 (04-srs:367); owner answer 2026-11-04 "page views, bookings, API calls" | Every CloudFront request counts (HTML, images, CSS, JS, fonts) | Count page views (HTML document requests) plus API calls; static files are not counted | approved | D3 answer 'Page views + API calls (recommended)', 2026-11-04; scope as in C2-A1 Accepted scope |

Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (page views + API calls) | B (every file) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| What CloudFront requests count | Rule #3 | all requests on tenant hosts | only HTML document requests (page views) on tenant hosts | all requests |
| API requests | Rule #3 | counted by API middleware | unchanged | unchanged |
| Unit size | Rule #3 | 1 unit = 1,000 requests | 1 unit = 1,000 page views and API calls | 1 unit = 1,000 requests |
| What an owner can check | none | not forecastable | close to visits in their own analytics | not forecastable |
| WAF and monitoring exclusions | Rule #3 | excluded | unchanged | unchanged |
| Tests | none | none | page with 30 assets counts 1; API call counts 1; asset-only request counts 0 | every fetch counts 1 |

## currentDecision (C2-A1)
Question: D3 — C2-A1: Should a "request" mean a page view or API call, rather than every file the browser fetches?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, CEO review 2 Section 1, Pricing Rule #3 request meter and FR-PL-066.
ELI10: When a visitor opens one page, the browser downloads the page plus dozens of small files: photos, styles, scripts, fonts. The plan counts every one of those as a request, so one visit can be 30 or more requests, and a theme with more photos costs the owner more without them knowing why. When you chose the request meter yesterday, the option described it as page views, bookings and API calls. Counting only pages and API calls makes the number match what an owner sees in Google Analytics.
Stakes if we pick wrong: owners see unit counts many times larger than their visitor numbers, and the first bills turn into disputes.
Recommendation: A because it matches the meter you picked and gives owners a number they can check against their own visitor counts (meter what customers can forecast).
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons:
A) Page views + API calls (recommended)
  ✅ A unit roughly equals visits the owner can see in their own analytics, so bills are explainable
  ✅ Theme and photo choices no longer change the bill behind the owner's back
  ❌ SSPL's CloudFront cost for images and files is not billed directly, so slab prices must cover it
B) Every file fetched
  ✅ Follows CloudFront's own request count, which is what AWS charges SSPL for
  ✅ No need to tell page requests apart from file requests in the logs
  ❌ Unit counts run dozens of times higher than visits and change with theme and photos
Net: an owner-explainable meter vs one that mirrors AWS's request count.
Header: Request meter
A) Page views + API calls (recommended)
Count only HTML document requests (page views) from CloudFront logs on the tenant's hosts, plus API calls counted by the API; images, CSS, JS, fonts and other static files are not counted. 1 unit = 1,000 page views and API calls. Tests: a page with 30 files counts 1; an API call counts 1; a file-only request counts 0. Effort S (human: ~2h / CC: ~15min). Low risk.
B) Every file fetched
Keep Rule #3 as written: every CloudFront request on the tenant's hosts counts, plus API calls. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: opaque, inflated unit counts.

State: approved
Actual answer: D3 answered 'Page views + API calls (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Request units count only HTML document requests (page views) from CloudFront logs on the tenant's hosts plus API calls counted by the API; images, CSS, JS, fonts and other static files are not counted; 1 unit = 1,000 page views and API calls. Rule #3 and the pack updated. Tests: a page with 30 files counts 1; an API call counts 1; a file-only request counts 0.
History: none

Other Section 1 notes: no new coupling beyond PlatformBilling reading UsageDaily; scaling at 10x/100x tenants: the log job and the single app server are the first limits (log volume grows with page views; one server CPU for API + renderer). Rollback: rate card versions are append-only, so a bad price is fixed by a new version from the next 1st, never by editing a used one. Dispositions: A1 accepted (D3).

### Section 2: Error and rescue map

```
  METHOD/CODEPATH                    | WHAT CAN GO WRONG                                  | CLASS
  -----------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------|------------------------------
  LogMeteringJob (daily)             | a day's CloudFront logs missing or late            | LogsIncompleteException
                                     | host maps to no tenant                             | (counted as unmapped, logged)
                                     | job crashes mid-day                                | (idempotent rerun, NFR-MTR)
  ApiRequestCounter (batched)        | batch flush fails, app server restarts             | CounterFlushException
  StorageSnapshotJob (daily)         | S3 inventory not ready                             | SnapshotMissingException
  MonthlyRating (on the 1st)         | a day has no completed metering run for a meter    | (see C2-A2)
                                     | no rate card version in force                      | RateCardMissingException
                                     | Razorpay link creation fails                       | PaymentLinkException
  WalletDebit (per message)          | balance below floor                                | WalletFloorReached (OQ23)
  RateCardPublish                    | effective date not a future 1st                    | RateCardDateException (400)
  -----------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------|------------------------------
  CLASS                      | RESCUED? | RESCUE ACTION                                   | USER SEES
  LogsIncompleteException    | ?        | see C2-A2                                       | see C2-A2
  CounterFlushException      | Y        | keep in-memory batch, retry with backoff; flush | nothing; counts land late
                             |          | on shutdown; alert if > 15 min behind           |
  SnapshotMissingException   | Y        | retry daily; use previous day's snapshot, logged| nothing
  RateCardMissingException   | Y        | hold all invoices, alert SSPL Finance           | invoice a day late
  PaymentLinkException       | Y        | retry; invoice issued with "pay from billing    | owner pays from billing page
                             |          | page" fallback                                  |
  RateCardDateException      | Y        | reject publish with message                     | Super Admin: "Rate cards start
                             |          |                                                 | on a future 1st of the month"
```
Dependent correction (follows Rule #4, no question): publishing a rate card version for a month that has already started is rejected; used versions are read-only.

Finding E1 `[P1] (8/10)`: MonthlyRating sums whatever UsageDaily rows exist (FR-PL-060, 04-srs:361). If a day's CloudFront logs never arrived, the log job failed, or the API counter lost a batch, the invoice silently undercounts and nobody knows; if the job is rerun late, a second invoice or a changed invoice appears. Pack OQ28 (late logs) is open. Decision row C2-A2.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2-A2, product owner (Section 2) | FR-PL-060 (04-srs:361); NFR-MTR (07 §12); OQ28 (13) | Rating on the 1st uses whatever usage exists | Rating checks every IST day of the month has a completed metering run per source; if not, it holds invoices and alerts | approved | D4 answer 'Check every day before billing (recommended)', 2026-11-04; scope as in C2-A2 Accepted scope |

Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (completeness check) | B (issue as is) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Before rating | FR-PL-060 | none | each IST day of the month has a completed run for logs, API counts and storage | none |
| If a day is missing | none | invoice undercounts silently | invoices held, SSPL Finance alerted, retried daily; invoices go out once complete | invoice undercounts silently |
| Invoice date | Rule #5 | the 1st | the 1st, or the day the month's data is complete (late invoices say why) | the 1st |
| Late logs (OQ28) | open | open | answered: rating waits for the month's last log day | open |
| Tests | none | none | missing day holds invoices and alerts; complete month issues on the 1st; rerun is idempotent | none |

## currentDecision (C2-A2)
Question: D4 — C2-A2: Should the monthly bill wait until every day's usage has been counted?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, CEO review 2 Section 2, MonthlyRating (FR-PL-060) and late CloudFront logs (OQ28).
ELI10: On the 1st, the system adds up each tenant's daily usage and sends the bill. If one day's data is missing, for example AWS delivered that day's logs late or a counting job crashed, the bill silently comes out too low, and nobody notices. A check that every day of the month was counted before billing, and a hold plus an alert when one wasn't, stops wrong bills from going out.
Stakes if we pick wrong: SSPL under-bills without knowing, or fixes it later with a corrected invoice that confuses the owner.
Recommendation: A because a bill should never go out on partial data without anyone knowing (zero silent failures), and it also settles the late-logs question.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=4/10
Pros / cons:
A) Check every day before billing (recommended)
  ✅ No invoice goes out with a missing day; SSPL Finance is alerted the same morning
  ✅ Answers the late-log question: the bill simply waits for the last day's logs
  ❌ Some months' invoices may go out a day or two after the 1st, with a note saying why
B) Bill on the 1st with whatever is counted
  ✅ Invoices always go out on the 1st, on schedule
  ✅ No completeness tracking to build
  ❌ A missing day under-bills silently, and late logs fall into nobody's month
Net: bills that are always complete vs bills that are always on time.
Header: Rating check
A) Check every day before billing (recommended)
Each metering source (log job, API counter flush, storage snapshot) records a completed run per IST day; MonthlyRating issues invoices only when all days of the month are complete, otherwise holds them, alerts SSPL Finance and retries daily; invoices issued late say why; reruns are idempotent. Tests: a missing day holds invoices and alerts; a complete month issues on the 1st; a rerun never duplicates. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) Bill on the 1st as is
Keep FR-PL-060: rate whatever UsageDaily holds on the 1st. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: silent under-billing.

State: approved
Actual answer: D4 answered 'Check every day before billing (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Each metering source (log job, API counter flush, storage snapshot) records a completed run per IST day; MonthlyRating issues invoices only when every day of the month is complete for every source, otherwise holds them, alerts SSPL Finance and retries daily; invoices issued after the 1st carry a note saying why; reruns are idempotent. Answers pack OQ28. Tests: a missing day holds invoices and alerts; a complete month issues on the 1st; a rerun never duplicates.
History: none

Section 2 dispositions: E1 accepted (D4). Remaining rescue rows are standard retries and alerts with user-visible messages; no catch-all handlers.

### Section 3: Security and threat model

| Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| A Super Admin account changes slab prices for every tenant | Low | High | Yes: SSO + MFA + SSPL IP range (FR-SA-001); rate card versions are append-only, audited and start only on a future 1st (Section 2 correction); Finance has view-only access |
| Tenant A reads tenant B's usage detail or invoices | Low | High | Yes: usage and invoice endpoints are tenant-scoped under RLS (eng R3) |
| Raw CloudFront logs hold visitor IP addresses | High | Med | Partly: pack OQ33 (retention) is open and needs counsel before launch |
| Someone inflates a rival tenant's request units with scripted page views | Low | Med | Partly: WAF rate rules exclude blocked requests; pack OQ22 (bot billing) is open |
| Wallet debit race at a slab boundary charges the wrong price | Med | Low | Yes: count and debit in one atomic step (eng R4, NFR-MTR) |

No new decisions: the two partial items are already open questions owned by the product owner (OQ22, OQ33).

### Section 4: Data flow and interaction edge cases

| Interaction | Edge case | Handled? | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rate card publish | effective date in the current month | Yes | rejected (Section 2 correction) |
| Rate card publish | two admins publish versions for the same month | Yes | one version per effective month (unique constraint); second publish rejected |
| Wallet debit | two messages cross a slab boundary at once | Yes | atomic count + debit (R4) |
| Trial ends mid-month | which days count toward slabs | Open | pack OQ27 |
| Tenant cancels mid-month | final server invoice | Yes | rating on the 1st covers every tenant live for any part of the month |
| Rating rerun | duplicate invoices | Yes | idempotent per tenant and month (C2-A2) |
| Billing page | estimate after a missing day | Yes | estimate shows "usage counted up to <date>" from the completed-run records (C2-A2) |

### Section 5: Code quality

No issues found. PlatformBilling owns metering, rating, invoices and the wallet; the request meter rule (C2-A1) lives in one place, the log job's filter, with the API counter as the only other producer.

Every row is required proof of an approved rule or answer; no pending test choices.

### Section 7: Performance

Finding P1 `[P2] (6/10)`, no question: the daily log job and rating run on the single app server (Rule #11) alongside the API and booking app. At launch scale the logs are small, but the implementation owner must prove the log job runs off-peak (night IST), streams files rather than loading a day into memory, and stays under a set time budget at 10x tenants; otherwise move it to Athena. Recorded as a task, not a scope change.

### Section 8: Observability

SSPL alerts: invoices held (C2-A2), metering behind by more than a day, API counter flush lag, app server health (Rule #11), wallet providers failing.

Finding O1 `[P1] (8/10)`: owners have no warning before a large bill. The billing page shows a daily estimate (Rule #5), but nothing tells an owner who isn't looking that this month is running far above last month, which is the main cause of bill shock in metered billing (Stripe, Dynamic Business, Rework guides). Wallet balance has a low-balance path; server usage has none. Decision row C2-A3.

| ID and owner | Contract and evidence | Current | Proposed | Status | Exact approval and scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2-A3, product owner (Section 8) | Rule #5 daily estimate (design:68); landscape sources in Step 0 | Estimate on the billing page only | Owner-set usage alert: email + WhatsApp when the month-to-date server estimate passes an amount the owner chooses, plus a banner in Tenant Admin | approved | D5 answer 'Owner-set usage alert (recommended)', 2026-11-04; scope as in C2-A3 Accepted scope |

Commitment comparison:
| Commitment | Source/approval or pending | Current | A (owner alert) | B (no alert) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Warning before a large server bill | none | none | owner sets an amount; alert at 80% and 100% of it, once each per month | none |
| Default when the owner sets nothing | none | n/a | alert when the estimate passes last month's bill by 50% | n/a |
| Channel | Notifications | n/a | email (always) + WhatsApp, sent as platform messages, not charged to the tenant wallet | n/a |
| Blocking | none | none | never blocks the website or bookings | none |
| Tests | none | none | threshold crossing sends once; no repeat the same month; default rule with no setting | none |

## currentDecision (C2-A3)
Question: D5 — C2-A3: Should owners get a warning when this month's bill is heading higher than they expect?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, CEO review 2 Section 8, Pricing Rule #5 daily estimate and bill-shock risk.
ELI10: With metered pricing, a busy month or a burst of visitors means a bigger bill. The plan shows a daily estimate on the billing page, but most owners won't check it. A simple alert, sent when this month's estimate passes an amount the owner picks (or 50% above last month if they pick nothing), means no owner is surprised on the 1st. It never stops their website or bookings; it only warns.
Stakes if we pick wrong: owners get a surprise bill, feel cheated, dispute it or leave, which is the most common failure of metered pricing.
Recommendation: A because a warning before the bill is the standard fix for bill shock and costs about a day to build (zero silent failures, for the owner too).
Completeness: A=10/10, B=5/10
Pros / cons:
A) Owner-set usage alert (recommended)
  ✅ Owners hear about a big month while it's happening, not on the 1st
  ✅ Works with no setup: a default alert at 50% above last month's bill
  ❌ About a day of work, plus alert messages SSPL pays for rather than the tenant wallet
B) No alert, billing page only
  ✅ Nothing extra to build before launch
  ✅ The daily estimate is already on the billing page for owners who look
  ❌ Owners who don't check the page find out from the invoice, the classic bill-shock case
Net: about a day of work vs owners learning about big months from the invoice.
Header: Usage alert
A) Owner-set usage alert (recommended)
Owners set a monthly server amount in Tenant Admin; when the month-to-date estimate passes 80% and 100% of it, send one email + WhatsApp each (platform messages, not charged to the wallet) and show a banner; with no amount set, alert once when the estimate passes last month's bill by 50%; never blocks the site or bookings. Tests: threshold crossing sends once; no repeat in the same month; default rule works with no setting. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Low risk.
B) No alert
Keep Rule #5: the daily estimate on the billing page only. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: bill shock.

State: approved
Actual answer: D5 answered 'Owner-set usage alert (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Owners set a monthly server amount in Tenant Admin; when the month-to-date estimate passes 80% and 100% of it, one email + WhatsApp each (platform messages, not charged to the wallet) and a Tenant Admin banner; with no amount set, one alert when the estimate passes last month's bill by 50%; never blocks the site or bookings. Tests: threshold crossing sends once; no repeat in the same month; default rule works with no setting.
History: none

Section 8 dispositions: O1 accepted (D5). Pack updated: FR-TA-181 usage alert.

### Section 9: Deployment and rollout

- One app server (Rule #11): each deploy restarts the API, admin, booking app and worker together; downtime per deploy is open (pack OQ25). Run deploys outside IST business hours until it is answered.
- Before launch: the first rate card version must be published with an effective date of the launch month's 1st (launch checklist item already in 12 §4). The rating job's first run covers the launch month.
- Rate card changes: append-only versions starting on a future 1st (Section 2 correction); rollback of a bad price is a new version from the next 1st.
- Rollback of the metering jobs: they are idempotent, so a fixed job reruns over affected days before rating (C2-A2 completeness records show which days).
- Post-deploy check (first hour after launch): a page view on a test tenant appears in the next day's UsageDaily; an API call is counted; a wallet debit lands at the slab-1 price.

### Section 10: Long-term trajectory

- Debt: request metering depends on CloudFront log format and the HTML-request filter (C2-A1); a theme that loads pages through client-side navigation could change page-view counts, so the filter needs a test per theme.
- Reversibility: 4/5. Rate cards are versioned; meters can be added later; the single server can become a pool behind the same managed database.
- Superseded records corrected by this review: the 2026-11-03 decision-log entry deferring billing and the learning "billing-not-in-launch-scope" (both replaced in the logs).
- 1-year question: a new engineer can follow the meters, slabs and completeness rule from Rules #3-#6, 04 FR-PL-060/064/066-069 and 07 NFR-MTR.

| Billing page | skeleton | "No usage yet this month" | "Couldn't load usage. Retry" | units, slabs, estimate | "Usage counted up to <date>" |
| Wallet page | skeleton | "Top up to send SMS and WhatsApp" | top-up failed message | balance, month-to-date per channel | below zero: "Messages are falling back to email" |
| Usage alert setting | saving | "No alert set: we'll warn you at 50% above last month" | validation message | saved amount | n/a |
| Rate card editor (SA) | saving | "No version yet" | "Rate cards start on a future 1st" | published version | draft not published |

Finding U1 `[P2] (7/10)`, no question: owners have never seen a slab bill for a website. The billing page and the pricing page need a worked example in plain words ("first 10 units at ₹X, next 40 at ₹Y") next to each slab table, the way the Andhra Pradesh discoms added leaflets. Built with DESIGN.md (22A); recommended for /plan-design-review.

## Re-review 3 decision ledger

### R22: where API calls are counted for the request meter
Finding: R22, P1, confidence 8/10, docs/requirements/04-software-requirements-specification.md FR-PL-066 ("API middleware counts requests per resolved tenant (FR-PL-001) and writes the counts to UsageDaily in batches") with 08-architecture.md:58 ("CF1 -->|"/api/*"| ALB"); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 3
Plan baseline: Rule #3 (2026-11-04) and C2-A1 (D3, 2026-11-04): request units = page views + API calls, each counted once; FR-PL-066 counts API calls in middleware and skips /api/* in the log job
Runtime evidence: no code. Two problems: (1) in-memory middleware counts on the single app server are lost on a crash or restart, and C2-A2's completeness check cannot see that loss; (2) "per resolved tenant" also counts the owner's own Tenant Admin and Super Admin/support API traffic (pack OQ34 open), so owners pay for managing their own space.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R22 API call source | middleware counter, batched to DB | CloudFront logs: `/api/*` requests on the tenant's website and booking-app hosts, in the same daily log job as page views | middleware counter with a durable local spool file flushed to DB; daily completed-run record per day | middleware counter as written |
| R22 admin traffic (OQ34) | counted (any request resolved to the tenant) | not counted: only customer-facing tenant hosts are metered | counted | counted |
| R22 crash loss | silent loss | none (logs are durable in S3) | none after spool replay | silent loss |
| C2-A2 completeness | log job + counter flush + storage | log job + storage | log job + spool flush + storage | log job + counter flush + storage |
| Components | log job + middleware counter | log job only (ApiRequestCounter removed) | log job + counter + spool | log job + counter |
| R22 tests | none | `/api/*` on a tenant host counts 1; admin-host API call counts 0; same request never counted twice | crash mid-batch replays spool; counts match | none |
Question D2:
D2 — R22: Where should API calls be counted for the server bill?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, eng re-review 3 of the pricing revision, FR-PL-066 request metering and pack OQ34.
ELI10: API calls are the behind-the-scenes requests the booking app makes, like checking free desks. The plan counts them inside the app server's memory, so if that one server restarts, the counts since the last save vanish and the bill silently comes out low. It also counts the owner's own admin screens, so owners would pay for managing their space. Those same booking-app calls already pass through CloudFront, which writes them to its logs, so counting them there with the page views is safer and simpler.
Stakes if we pick wrong: bills silently miss usage after a server restart, or owners get billed for using their own admin screens.
Recommendation: A because the logs are already durable and already read daily, so one source covers page views and API calls with nothing lost on restart, and only customer traffic is billed.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=8/10, C=4/10
Pros / cons:
A) Count from CloudFront logs (recommended)
  ✅ Nothing is lost when the app server restarts, because CloudFront logs are saved in S3
  ✅ Owners are billed only for their customers' traffic, never for their own admin screens
  ❌ API calls show up on the bill a day later, with the daily log run, not in real time
B) Counter with a durable spool
  ✅ Counts survive a restart because they are written to a local file before batching
  ✅ Can count any API traffic, including admin screens, if that is ever wanted
  ❌ A second metering path to build and test, and owners still pay for admin traffic
C) Keep the in-memory counter
  ✅ No change to the pack as written
  ✅ Simple to build: one counter in the API
  ❌ A restart silently loses counts, and admin use is billed
Net: one durable log source for customer traffic vs a second counting path.
Header: API counting
Options:
A) Count from CloudFront logs (recommended)
The daily log job counts `/api/*` requests on the tenant's website and booking-app hosts from CloudFront standard logs, alongside page views; the API middleware counter is removed; Tenant Admin, Super Admin and support traffic is not metered (answers OQ34). Tests: a tenant-host API call counts 1; an admin-host API call counts 0; no request is counted twice. Effort S (human: ~2h / CC: ~15min). Low risk.
B) Counter with a durable spool
Keep the middleware counter but write counts to a local spool file before batching to the DB, replayed after a restart, with a completed-run record per day for C2-A2; admin traffic stays counted. Tests: crash mid-batch replays the spool. Effort S (human: ~1 day / CC: ~1h). Medium risk.
C) Keep the in-memory counter
Keep FR-PL-066 as written. Effort S (zero implementation change). High risk: silent under-billing after restarts.

State: approved
Actual answer: D2 answered 'Count from CloudFront logs (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: The daily log job counts `/api/*` requests on the tenant's website and booking-app hosts from CloudFront standard logs, alongside page views; the API middleware counter is removed; Tenant Admin, Super Admin and support traffic is not metered (answers pack OQ34). Tests: a tenant-host API call counts 1; an admin-host API call counts 0; no request is counted twice.
History: none

### R21: which responses count as billable page views and API calls
Finding: R21, P2, confidence 7/10, design Rule #3 ("page views are HTML document requests in the CloudFront logs ... API calls ...") and C2-A1 accepted scope; reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 3
Plan baseline: C2-A1 (D3) page views = HTML document requests; R22 (D2 of this review) API calls = `/api/*` on tenant hosts from CloudFront logs; status codes unspecified
Runtime evidence: no code. CloudFront standard logs record the status (sc-status) and content type (sc-content-type) per request. Without a rule, 404 pages, 301 redirects (FR-TW custom-domain canonicalisation) and 5xx errors caused by SSPL's own outages would all count toward the tenant's bill.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R21 billable statuses | unspecified (all non-WAF-blocked) | 2xx and 304 only | every status except WAF-blocked |
| Redirects (301/302) | counted | not counted | counted |
| Not found (404) and other 4xx | counted | not counted | counted |
| Server errors (5xx, SSPL's fault) | counted | not counted | counted |
| C2-A1 page-view filter, R22 source | approved | unchanged | unchanged |
| R21 tests | none | 200 page counts 1; 304 counts 1; 301, 404, 503 count 0 | every status counts 1 |
Question D3:
D3 — R21: Should failed and redirected requests be left off the server bill?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, eng re-review 3 of the pricing revision, request meter status codes.
ELI10: Not every request a visitor makes succeeds. Some hit a "page not found", some are redirected to the right web address, and some fail because SSPL's server had a problem. If those count, an owner pays for broken links and even for SSPL's own outages. Counting only requests that actually delivered a page or an answer keeps the bill fair and easy to defend.
Stakes if we pick wrong: owners are billed more during an SSPL outage, which turns a bad day into a dispute.
Recommendation: A because an owner should only pay for pages and answers that were actually delivered, and never for SSPL's own errors.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=5/10
Pros / cons:
A) Count successful responses only (recommended)
  ✅ Owners never pay for SSPL outages, broken links or address redirects
  ✅ Easy to explain on a bill: only pages and answers that were delivered count
  ❌ SSPL carries the cost of serving errors and redirects, a small share of traffic
B) Count every non-blocked request
  ✅ Simplest log rule: no status filter at all
  ✅ Covers SSPL's cost for every request it serves
  ❌ An outage or a broken link raises the tenant's bill, inviting disputes
Net: a fair, defensible bill vs the simplest filter.
Header: Billable status
Options:
A) Successful responses only (recommended)
Count page views and `/api/*` calls only when the response status is 2xx or 304; redirects (3xx), client errors (4xx) and server errors (5xx) are not billed. Tests: a 200 page counts 1; a 304 counts 1; 301, 404 and 503 count 0. Effort S (human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Low risk.
B) Every non-blocked request
Count every page view and API call that WAF did not block, whatever its status. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: bills rise with errors and outages.

State: approved
Actual answer: D3 answered 'Successful responses only (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Page views and `/api/*` calls count only when the response status is 2xx or 304; redirects (3xx), client errors (4xx) and server errors (5xx) are not billed. Tests: a 200 page counts 1; a 304 counts 1; 301, 404 and 503 count 0.
History: none

### R23: when a notification message is charged to the wallet
Finding: R23, P2, confidence 7/10, docs/requirements/04-software-requirements-specification.md FR-PL-042 ("for each message, one transaction increments the tenant's MonthlyMeterCount for the channel ... debits the wallet") with Rule #6; reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 3
Plan baseline: Rule #6 (2026-11-04) prepaid wallet, slab price by month-to-date count, atomic count + debit (R4); FR-PL-042 debits at send time; nothing says what happens when a message is rejected or never delivered
Runtime evidence: no code. Providers differ: WhatsApp Cloud API bills SSPL for delivered template messages; an SMS rejected by DLT scrubbing before submission is not billed; SES bills each email sent. Delivery reports arrive asynchronously, after the debit.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R23 when charged | at send (FR-PL-042) | at send (count + debit atomic, R4) | at send |
| Rejected or undelivered | unspecified | credited back (wallet credit + count decremented) only when the provider does not charge SSPL: WhatsApp not delivered within 24h, SMS rejected before submission (DLT); emails never credited | never credited |
| Slab position after a credit | n/a | count goes down by one; later messages priced from the corrected count | unchanged |
| Wallet history | WalletTransaction per debit | plus a linked credit transaction with the provider's reason | debits only |
| R23 tests | none | DLT rejection credits once; WhatsApp undelivered after 24h credits once; duplicate delivery report credits nothing more | none |
Question D4:
D4 — R23: Should a tenant get a wallet credit when a message is never delivered?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, eng re-review 3 of the pricing revision, FR-PL-042 wallet debits and Rule #6.
ELI10: The wallet is charged the moment a message is sent. Sometimes the message never arrives: the SMS gateway rejects it for a template problem, or a WhatsApp message can't reach the phone. In some of those cases the provider doesn't charge SSPL either. Giving the tenant the money back in exactly those cases means the tenant pays for what SSPL pays for, no more and no less.
Stakes if we pick wrong: owners pay for messages that never arrived and SSPL never paid for, or SSPL refunds messages it did pay for.
Recommendation: A because crediting only what the provider doesn't charge keeps "charged as consumed" honest without SSPL losing money on refunds.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=6/10
Pros / cons:
A) Credit undelivered messages the provider didn't charge (recommended)
  ✅ The wallet matches what was actually delivered and paid for, message by message
  ✅ SSPL never refunds money it already paid to a provider
  ❌ Delivery reports must be matched to debits, and slab counts can go down after a credit
B) No credits: charge every send
  ✅ Simplest: one debit per send and nothing to undo later
  ✅ The month's slab counts only ever go up
  ❌ Owners pay for messages that never arrived, and SSPL keeps money it didn't spend
Net: an honest per-message bill vs the simplest wallet.
Header: Message credits
Options:
A) Credit undelivered, unbilled messages (recommended)
Charge at send (count + debit atomic, R4); when a delivery report shows the provider did not charge SSPL (WhatsApp not delivered within 24h, SMS rejected by DLT before submission), credit the wallet, decrement the month's count and write a linked credit with the reason; emails are never credited. Tests: a DLT rejection credits once; an undelivered WhatsApp after 24h credits once; a duplicate report credits nothing more. Effort S (human: ~half day / CC: ~30min). Low risk.
B) No credits
Keep FR-PL-042: every send is charged, whatever happens to the message. Effort S (zero implementation change). Medium risk: owners pay for undelivered messages.

State: approved
Actual answer: D4 answered 'Credit undelivered, unbilled messages (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Charge at send (count + debit atomic, R4); when a delivery report shows the provider did not charge SSPL (WhatsApp not delivered within 24h, SMS rejected by DLT before submission), credit the wallet, decrement the month's count and write a linked credit with the reason; emails are never credited. Tests: a DLT rejection credits once; an undelivered WhatsApp after 24h credits once; a duplicate report credits nothing more.
History: none

### Re-review 3 Section 1: Architecture

```
  Tenant website / booking-app hosts ──► CloudFront ──► standard logs (S3) ──► MeteringJobs.log (daily, off-peak)
        │ /api/* ──► ALB ──► one app server                                    │ page views (text/html) + /api/*
        │                                                                     │ 2xx/304 only, WAF + monitoring excluded
  S3 media ──► MeteringJobs.storage (daily snapshot) ───────────────────────────┤
                                                                               ▼
                                                         UsageDaily + completed-run record per IST day per source
                                                                               │
              SlabCalculator (one implementation) ◄── RateCardService (versions from a future 1st)
                 │            │             │
   BillingDailyPass:       MonthlyRating    WalletDebit (per message; count + debit atomic)
   estimate + alert        (complete month       │
   (FR-TA-181)              only, C2-A2)          └──► delivery report ──► credit if provider didn't charge (R23)
```
Findings: `[P1] (8/10)` FR-PL-066 in-memory API counter loses counts on restart and bills admin traffic (R22, accepted D2). Production failure: CloudFront log delivery stops for a distribution → C2-A2 holds invoices and alerts. Dispositions: R22 accepted.

### Re-review 3 Section 4: Performance

Finding `[P2] (6/10)`, no question: the renderer (Node) and the API share the one app server (Rule #11); a burst of publishes or data refreshes can raise API p95 for every tenant's booking app. Implementation owner must cap renderer concurrency (one job at a time, lower CPU priority) and alarm on API p95. The log job runs off-peak and streams (C2T5). At launch scale (tens of tenants) the logs are small; a 10x load test is required before 200 tenants.

# Design Review 2 (plan-design-review, 2026-11-04, billing UI after the pricing revision)

Scope (D1, answered 2026-11-04): all 7 passes on the pricing page, billing page, wallet page, usage alert, rate card editor, cost view, sign-up and wizard, plus the 6 gaps left open by the first design review. Text-only: the gstack designer needs an OpenAI key that isn't set up. Outside voice (D2): native Claude design subagent only (Codex not installed); its findings are folded into the passes below. Factual correction without a question: FR-MW-003's "1 request unit = 1,000 requests" now reads "1,000 page views and API calls" (C2-A1).

## Design review 2: approved decisions

Each was asked separately and answered by the product owner on 2026-11-04.

**DR2-1A Billing page order (FR-TA-175).** (1) Status line: "Nothing due" or "₹X due by <date> · Pay"; (2) "This month so far": server estimate + wallet spend as one total "+ GST"; (3) per-meter detail with slab breakdown; (4) wallet balance and link; (5) "0% commission: ₹0 on ₹<bookings> of bookings"; (6) invoice history.

**DR2-2A Pricing page leads with sample bills (FR-MW-002/003).** After the 0% commission promise, 2-3 sample monthly bills for typical space profiles (seats, page views, bookings, messages), each computed by the SlabCalculator from the rate card in force, shown as total + GST with a one-line breakdown; SSPL sets the profiles in the rate card editor; the six slab tables sit below in folded sections.

**DR2-3A Billing and wallet states (adds to CEO-2 Section 11 table).**

| State | User sees | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Invoice being prepared (C2-A2 hold) | "Your <month> bill is being prepared · usage counted up to <date>" | none |
| Payment pending confirmation | "Payment received by Razorpay · confirming" | none; refreshes |
| Autopay debit failed | "Autopay for ₹X didn't go through · <reason>" | Retry · Pay by link |
| Trial | "Server usage not billed until <date>" | Add payment method |
| First partial month | "Billed from <date>" on the invoice | none |
| Upcoming rate card | "New prices from 1 <month>" | See changes |
| Credited message (R23) | wallet row "Credit · WhatsApp not delivered" | none |
| Estimate copy | "estimate · final when <month> closes" (replaces "final on the 1st") | none |

**DR2-4A Wallet runway and early alert (FR-TA-180).** "About N days left at this month's rate" from the last 7 days' spend; email + WhatsApp alert (platform message, not charged) when runway falls below 5 days, once per top-up cycle; each channel shows its current slab price and "next price from message N".

**DR2-5A Usage alert details (FR-TA-181).** The amount is server usage only, before GST, labelled "Server usage, before GST"; the setting suggests this month's estimate rounded up to the next ₹100; in month 1 the default rule shows "We'll start alerting you after your first bill"; the banner links to "This month so far" and can be dismissed for the rest of the month.

**DR2-6A Usage shown as recognisable counts (FR-TA-175).** Per request meter: "Page views 2,140 · Booking app activity 1,272 = 3,412 → 3.41 units"; storage "2.3 GB average"; Indian digit grouping (1,00,000); money to paise; units to 2 decimals pending OQ26.

**DR2-7A Rate card publish safety (FR-SA-020).** Save draft, duplicate from the version in force, delete draft; side-by-side diff with changed slabs highlighted; impact preview that re-rates the last complete month's usage with the draft (tenants paying more/less, median and largest change, total); publish requires typing the effective month (for example "JAN 2027").

**DR2-8A Cost view leads with margin (FR-SA-022).** 12-month row of billed revenue (server + wallet), actual cost (AWS + messaging providers), margin ₹ and %; below, revenue per meter beside the closest cost lines (requests ↔ CloudFront + app server; storage ↔ S3; each channel ↔ its provider), labelled "related cost, not exact".

**DR2-9A Phone layout and accessible tables.** Below 600px each slab is a stacked row "<range> · ₹<price> each · <used> used · ₹<amount>"; above 600px a real table with caption and th scope, right-aligned tabular numerals; amounts announced as rupees; 44px targets for Pay and Top up.

**DR2-10A Keyboard reorder in the builder (FR-TA-023, first-review gap).** Each section row has Move up / Move down buttons (44px); when focused, Alt+Arrow moves it; a live region announces "<Section> moved to position N of M"; drag stays for mouse users.

**DR2-11A Wallet top-up GST (OQ32).** The owner picks or types the wallet amount (minimum ₹500); the sheet shows "You pay ₹<amount + 18% GST> · You get ₹<amount> of messages"; a GST invoice is issued per top-up; balances and debits stay before GST. Subject to CA confirmation.

**DR2-12A Refund-failed alert (US-8.1, first-review gap).** Today view pins "Refund of ₹X to <customer> failed · <gateway reason> · Retry" until resolved; detail page offers Retry and "Refunded another way" (UPI/bank reference required, audited); the customer gets "Your refund is delayed; <space> is on it" by email/WhatsApp; the payments list shows a Failed badge.

**DR2-13A Email OTP fallback (FR-BA-001, first-review gap).** When phone OTP can't be sent, the step shows "We'll send your code by email instead" with the email field focused (asks for one if none is on file); copy in the space's voice with no mention of wallets or SMS problems (29A); "Try SMS again" after 60 seconds.

**DR2-14A Website price caption (FR-TW-004, first-review gap).** Spaces & pricing sections show a small caption "Prices confirmed at checkout" under the "from" prices, in the tenant's voice; the 2A checkout re-quote stays.

**DR2-15A Navigation maps (first-review gap).** Super Admin left nav: Tenants (list, scorecard, demo requests) · Review queue · Billing (rate card, server invoices, rating runs, wallets) · Cost view · Support grants · Platform health · Settings. cowos.in top nav: Product · Pricing · Demo sites · Help · Sign in, plus a "Start free trial" button; footer: About SSPL, Contact, Legal.

Reused without a question: 22A (DESIGN.md in Sprint 0 via /design-consultation) settles the first review's sixth gap; the new components (slab table / stacked slab row, usage line, sample-bill row, runway line) join that DESIGN.md component list.

# Engineering Re-review 4 (plan-eng-review, 2026-11-04, after Design Review 2)

Target: docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md, "Design Review 2" (DR2-1A to DR2-15A, DR2T1-DR2T7). Earlier decisions (R3-R23, C2-A1-A3, re-review 3 D1) stand.

## Re-review 4 scope record

feature answers: none proposed; structure: A Smaller arrangement (D1, answered by the product owner 2026-11-04); accepted scope: wallet runway computed in BillingDailyPass; impact preview and sample bills in RateCardService; all slab maths through the SlabCalculator; UI changes in tenant-admin, superadmin, marketing and booking apps; no new services; pending remedies: R24, R25.

Scope Challenge result: scope accepted as-is (smaller arrangement).

## Re-review 4 decision ledger

### R24: manual "Refunded another way" while a gateway refund retry may still succeed
Finding: R24, P1, confidence 8/10, design DR2-12A ("detail page offers Retry and 'Refunded another way' (UPI/bank reference required, audited)") with FR-TA-094 and FR-PL-050 (webhooks); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 4
Plan baseline: DR2-12A (approved 2026-11-04) pinned alert, Retry, manual fallback; nothing orders a manual refund against an in-flight gateway retry
Runtime evidence: no code. Razorpay refunds can sit in "pending" and complete later; a retry can be accepted after staff have already paid the customer by UPI. The customer is then refunded twice and the space loses money.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R24 manual refund allowed when | any time | only when the gateway refund is in a final failed state and no retry is pending; Retry is disabled after a manual record | any time, with a warning that a pending retry may also pay out |
| Late gateway success after a manual record | unhandled | cannot happen (no retry pending) | flagged "Possible double refund" to staff with the amount to recover |
| Refund state | failed/retry | Failed → (Retrying → Failed or Refunded) or → RefundedManually; transitions guarded in one place | same states, no guard |
| R24 tests | none | manual record rejected while a retry is pending; retry rejected after a manual record | late success after a manual record raises the flag |
Question D2:
D2 — R24: When can staff mark a failed refund as "refunded another way"?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, eng re-review 4, refund-failed alert (DR2-12A).
ELI10: When a customer's refund fails, staff can retry it or pay the customer another way, for example by UPI, and record that. But a retried refund can sit as "pending" at Razorpay and go through later. If staff paid by UPI in the meantime, the customer gets the money twice. Allowing the manual option only when the gateway refund has definitely failed, and blocking further retries after a manual payment, makes a double refund impossible.
Stakes if we pick wrong: the space pays some customers twice and has to chase the money back.
Recommendation: A because a guard that makes double refunds impossible beats a warning staff can click past.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=6/10
Pros / cons:
A) Allow manual only after a final failure (recommended)
  ✅ A customer can never be refunded both by Razorpay and by staff
  ✅ Clear rule for staff: Retry or record manual, never both at once
  ❌ Staff wait for a pending retry to finish before they can pay another way
B) Allow anytime with a warning
  ✅ Staff can settle the customer immediately, without waiting
  ✅ A late gateway payout is still detected and flagged afterwards
  ❌ Double refunds happen and must be recovered from customers by hand
Net: impossible double refunds vs faster manual settlement.
Header: Manual refund
Options:
A) Manual only after final failure (recommended)
"Refunded another way" is enabled only when the gateway refund is in a final failed state with no retry pending; recording it disables Retry; refund state transitions (Failed, Retrying, Refunded, RefundedManually) are guarded in one place. Tests: manual record rejected while a retry is pending; retry rejected after a manual record. Effort S (human: ~2h / CC: ~15min). Low risk.
B) Anytime with a warning
"Refunded another way" is always available with a warning; a later gateway success after a manual record raises "Possible double refund" for staff with the amount to recover. Tests: late success raises the flag. Effort S (human: ~2h / CC: ~15min). Medium risk: double refunds.

State: approved
Actual answer: D2 answered 'Manual only after final failure (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: "Refunded another way" is enabled only when the gateway refund is in a final failed state with no retry pending; recording it disables Retry; refund state transitions (Failed, Retrying, Refunded, RefundedManually) are guarded in one place. Tests: manual record rejected while a retry is pending; retry rejected after a manual record.
History: none

### R25: email sign-in codes once a wallet is past the −₹500 floor
Finding: R25, P1, confidence 8/10, design DR2-13A ("When phone OTP can't be sent, the step shows 'We'll send your code by email instead'") with Pricing Rule #6 ("OTPs and emails continue down to a −₹500 floor") and pack OQ23 (open); reviewer: plan-eng-review re-review 4
Plan baseline: DR2-13A (approved 2026-11-04) promises a working email fallback; Rule #6 charges emails to the wallet and stops at −₹500; OQ23 leaves what stops open
Runtime evidence: no code. Past the floor, the SMS OTP fails and the email OTP is also refused by the wallet, so the "working path" in DR2-13A is a dead end and the customer cannot sign in to book.
Comparison grid:
| Choice | Current | A | B |
|---|---|---|---|
| R25 sign-in OTP email past the floor | refused (OQ23 open) | always sent; past the floor the cost is absorbed by SSPL and logged, not debited | refused; the step shows the space's phone and WhatsApp to book directly |
| Other emails past the floor | open (OQ23) | unchanged, still open | unchanged, still open |
| Owner signal | none | wallet page and alert note "Sign-in codes are still being sent; top up to restore SMS and WhatsApp" | wallet zero warning only |
| DR2-13A promise | broken past the floor | holds | replaced by a contact path |
| R25 tests | none | past the floor, a sign-in email OTP sends and is logged as absorbed, no debit | past the floor, the step shows contact options |
Question D3:
D3 — R25: Should customers still get email sign-in codes when the space's wallet is past its limit?
Project/branch/task: Cowos, eng re-review 4, email OTP fallback (DR2-13A) and wallet floor (Pricing Rule #6, OQ23).
ELI10: If a space lets its wallet run down past the −₹500 limit, SMS codes stop and the plan falls back to emailing the sign-in code. But emails are charged to the same wallet, so past the limit the email is refused too, and the customer can't sign in to book at all. Always sending the sign-in email (it costs SSPL a fraction of a paisa) keeps bookings possible while the owner is nudged to top up.
Stakes if we pick wrong: customers of a space with an empty wallet can't book, so the space loses business over a tiny messaging cost.
Recommendation: A because a customer's booking should never fail over a sub-paisa email, and the fallback you approved promised a working path.
Completeness: A=10/10, B=5/10
Pros / cons:
A) Always send sign-in code emails (recommended)
  ✅ Customers can always sign in and book, even when the owner's wallet is empty
  ✅ Keeps the email fallback you approved working in every case
  ❌ SSPL absorbs a very small email cost for spaces that stay past the limit
B) Stop and show the space's contact details
  ✅ SSPL never pays for a tenant's messages beyond the limit
  ✅ Pushes owners harder to keep the wallet topped up
  ❌ Customers hit a dead end at sign-in and most leave instead of calling
Net: bookings always possible vs strict wallet limits.
Header: OTP past floor
Options:
A) Always send sign-in emails (recommended)
Sign-in OTP emails are always sent; past the −₹500 floor their cost is absorbed by SSPL and logged, not debited; the wallet page and runway alert tell the owner "Sign-in codes are still being sent; top up to restore SMS and WhatsApp"; other messages past the floor stay with OQ23. Tests: past the floor a sign-in email OTP sends, is logged as absorbed and makes no debit. Effort S (human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Low risk.
B) Stop and show contact details
Past the floor the email OTP is refused and the sign-in step shows the space's phone and WhatsApp to book directly (replaces the DR2-13A fallback in that case). Tests: past the floor the step shows contact options. Effort S (human: ~1h / CC: ~10min). Medium risk: lost bookings.

State: approved
Actual answer: D3 answered 'Always send sign-in emails (recommended)' by the product owner, 2026-11-04
Accepted scope: Sign-in OTP emails are always sent; past the −₹500 floor their cost is absorbed by SSPL and logged, not debited; the wallet page and runway alert tell the owner "Sign-in codes are still being sent; top up to restore SMS and WhatsApp"; other messages past the floor stay with OQ23. Tests: past the floor a sign-in email OTP sends, is logged as absorbed and makes no debit.
History: none

### Re-review 4 Section 1: Architecture

No issues found. The DR2 additions reuse existing pieces: the SlabCalculator (sample bills, impact preview, runway price steps), BillingDailyPass (runway, alerts), RateCardService (drafts, preview, sample profiles stored with each version), CostImport (margin view), the 2A re-quote (website price caption), NotificationService (runway and refund-delay messages). Sample bill profiles are versioned with the rate card so the pricing page rebuild (FR-MW-013) always matches the published prices. Impact preview re-rates the last complete month for all tenants on demand (tens to hundreds of tenants, one SlabCalculator call per tenant and meter).

### Re-review 4 Section 2: Code quality

Findings: `[P1] (8/10)` DR2-12A manual refund can double-pay against a pending gateway retry (R24, accepted D2); `[P1] (8/10)` DR2-13A email fallback dead-ends past the wallet floor (R25, accepted D3). Dependent details (no question): runway uses the last 7 days' spend, or days since sign-up when fewer than 7; GST-on-top top-ups (DR2-11A) issue an SSPL GST invoice per top-up from the same invoice series as server invoices; DR2-10A reorder moves go through the same section-order command as drag, so undo and publish behave the same.

| Impact preview | draft has a typo price | preview shows large increases | typed publish confirm | SSPL sees impact before publishing |

# CEO Review 3 (plan-ceo-review, 2026-11-05): revision 2

Target: revision 2 of 2026-11-04 (head of this document; pack v0.4): the Fixed and Usage plans (Rule #12), seat maps at launch (Rule #13, "Seat maps (revision 2)"), and the small-team build plan `docs/requirements/14-build-plan.md` with a week-20 launch target. Review depth: implementation-ready for the revision; earlier reviewed decisions are carried, not re-opened.

## Step 0 evidence

### 0A. Premise challenge

The owner's real problems: (1) owners want a bill they can predict, (2) describing a space's seating is the hardest setup step, (3) every app must be finished before selling, as soon as possible. Do-nothing cost: a usage-only bill scares owners who want a fixed cost, a list-only seat picker loses to tools with floor plans, and a 28-week build delays all revenue.

| Premise | Claim | Receipt | Verdict |
|---------|-------|---------|---------|
| P-R2-1 | Server cost per small tenant is small (₹100–₹400/month at 200+ tenants), so "unlimited server use" on the Fixed plan is safe | docs/requirements/00-start-here.md:85 | [estimated] in the pack; plausible on one shared app server; the cost view (Rule #10) measures it per tenant after launch |
| P-R2-2 | Messages are SSPL's largest variable cost, so they need an allowance on the Fixed plan | docs/designs/cowos-platform-v1.md:79 (Rule #12) | Agreed; owner chose the fair-use cap |
| P-R2-3 | Owners find describing their seating hard; a grid builder with templates fixes it | design doc "Seat maps (revision 2)", line 118 | Owner's statement, unverified with owners. Landscape: coworking tools ship member-facing floor plans (Spacebring), office tools upload a floor image (Usecoord); a coworking forum thread names member booking from the map as hard to find. Grid-first setup speed is a credible differentiator |
| P-R2-4 | 4 developers using Claude Code reach launch in week 20 | docs/requirements/14-build-plan.md:5 | **Unverified.** The full-team plan is about 6 engineers × 25 weeks ≈ 150 person-weeks; week 20 with 4 developers is about 76 person-weeks, so it assumes roughly 2× output per developer. Published evidence on AI coding speed-up is moderate (a 2026 meta-analysis of 23 studies: Hedges' g = 0.33) and METR's randomized trials found no clear speed-up for experienced developers |
| P-R2-5 | Razorpay Partner approval arrives by week 15 | docs/requirements/14-build-plan.md:70 | Unverified; outside SSPL's control |
| P-R2-6 | Build the complete suite regardless of demand evidence | design doc Premise 3, line 56 | Owner's accepted risk (pack R26); not re-opened |

unverified=3 (P-R2-3, P-R2-4, P-R2-5).

### 0B. Existing leverage

Revision 2 reuses what is already designed: the rate card and its versions (FR-SA-020) carry the Fixed fee and allowances; the one SlabCalculator prices the Usage plan, the Fixed plan's over-allowance messages and the "other plan" comparison; the wallet's atomic count + debit (R4) handles over-allowance messages; seats are ProductUnits, so the existing booking exclusion constraint prevents double-booked seats; holds (FR-BA-017) cover picked seats. New pieces: TenantPlanPeriod, the plan choice/switch screen (FR-TA-182), the seat map builder and picker, SeatMap/SeatMapVersion.

### 0C. Dream state

```
  CURRENT STATE                    THIS PLAN (revision 2)                 12-MONTH IDEAL
  Requirements pack only;   --->   Two plans owners can compare;    --->  100+ paying spaces in 2 cities;
  no code; usage-only              seat map at launch; 6 apps             owners set up a space in under
  pricing; 28-week build           in weekly slices; week-20 target       30 minutes incl. seat map; most
                                                                          bookings direct, 0% commission
```

The plan moves toward the ideal. The gap is pace: the date depends on an unproven speed-up.

### Landscape (web search, 2026-11-05)

- [Layer 1] Coworking tools offer floor-plan booking (Spacebring interactive floor plans); office desk tools turn an uploaded floor image into a desk map (Usecoord). Hybrid pricing (fixed + usage) is common in SaaS; flat fees buy predictability, usage lowers the entry cost.
- [Layer 2] Vendor sources favour usage pricing for small businesses; flat-fee predictability is the main counter-argument. AI coding speed-up claims range from 55% (vendors) to none (METR).
- [Layer 3] For Indian independent spaces, letting the owner choose the plan beats picking one model for them, as long as the billing page shows the other plan's cost. For pace, the honest unit is measured sprint output from week 3, not a vendor speed-up figure.

Sources: [Spacebring floor plans](https://www.spacebring.com/features/floor-plans), [coworking.org forum](https://forum.coworking.org/t/management-systems-for-a-coworking-space/7363), [Usecoord](https://marketplace.microsoft.com/ar-eg/product/usecoord.usecoord), [Monetizely on usage vs flat](https://www.getmonetizely.com/faqs/what-are-the-pros-and-cons-of-usage-based-pricing-versus-flat-monthly-subscriptions-especially-for-an-early-stage-saas), [Younium on hybrid pricing](https://www.younium.com/blog/usage-based-pricing), [AI coding evidence summary](https://dev.to/vibehackers/ai-coding-tools-and-productivity-what-the-controlled-evidence-shows-1emf), [Bain view](https://tianpan.co/forum/t/ai-writes-41-of-our-code-vendors-claim-55-speedups-bain-says-gains-are-unremarkable-whos-measuring-wrong/1767).

## CEO review 3: review sections

Current scope: mode SCOPE REDUCTION (D1). Kept: R3-C1 (D2). Deferred: R3-C2 (D3), R3-C3 (D4), R3-C4 (D5), R3-C5 (D6). Rejected: none.

### Section 1: Architecture

```
                         cowos.in (marketing)
                               | sign-up, pricing (Fixed + Usage cards)
                               v
 Tenant Admin ----------> ASP.NET Core API (one app server) <---------- Booking app
  plan choice/switch       |  Tenancy: state + TenantPlanPeriod [new]      seat picker [new]
  seat map builder [new]   |  Inventory: SeatMap (draft/live) [new],       list view [new]
  "Change seat" [new]      |             seats = ProductUnit(kind=Seat)
                           |  Booking: availability per seat, holds,
                           |           exclusion constraint (reused)
                           |  PlatformBilling: rate card (+Fixed fee,
                           |     allowances) [changed], SlabCalculator
                           |     (reused), MonthlyRating by plan [changed],
                           |     wallet debit with allowance [changed]
                           v
                   PostgreSQL (RDS, RLS)          CloudFront logs -> metering jobs (all tenants)
Super Admin --> rate card editor (plain, R3-C3), tenant list, scorecard, dunning
```

Coupling: rating now reads TenantPlanPeriod as well as UsageDaily; the wallet debit reads the plan and allowance. Both stay inside PlatformBilling, so no new cross-module dependency. Seats reuse ProductUnit and BookingAllocation, so Booking does not depend on SeatMap (only Inventory does).

State machines:

```
TenantPlanPeriod (per tenant)
  [none] --activate(plan)--> OPEN(plan, starts_on=activation day)
  OPEN --schedule switch--> OPEN + FUTURE(other plan, starts_on=next 1st)
  FUTURE --cancel (before its 1st)--> removed
  FUTURE --1st arrives--> previous OPEN closes on last day; FUTURE becomes OPEN
  invalid: two OPEN periods; overlapping ranges (exclusion constraint); FUTURE starting on a non-1st;
           cancel after starts_on (rejected: "already started")

SeatMap
  [draft] --publish (validates)--> LIVE vN --edit--> LIVE vN + DRAFT --publish--> LIVE vN+1
  publish rejected when: unassigned seat, duplicate label, overlapping block,
                          a removed/reassigned seat or block with a future booking
```

Scaling: per seat-map request, one query over BookingAllocation for at most 2,400 seats in a time range, served by the existing GiST index; first to strain at 100× is the shared app server, unchanged from Rule #11.

Security: new mutations are Owner-only plan change (audited), Owner/Manager seat map publish, staff "Change seat". Public seat availability returns layout and states only, no customer data (10-api-modules).

Rollback: plan and seat map are greenfield and ship with launch; no live data to roll back.

Findings:
- **WARNING R3-A1. Turning seats on for a hot desk pool that already has future bookings.** Today a pool booking only increments DayCapacityLedger and has no seat (09-data-model.md:227). When the owner assigns seats and Seat choice becomes Optional (09-data-model.md:164), those future bookings have no seat, so the map shows every seat free and a customer can pick a seat that is already sold. Capacity math also changes from the ledger to seat allocations. The plan does not say what happens to existing seatless bookings.

## Answered: R3-A1 (D7)
Approved: auto-assign seats on publish ("Auto-assign seats on publish (recommended)", 2026-11-05). When a map publish turns Seat choice on (or adds seats) for a hot desk product, every future booking of that product without a seat gets a free seat by the "any available seat" rule in the same transaction; publish fails, listing the day, if any day has more bookings than seats; each customer gets the seat by email and WhatsApp and the desk pass updates. Test: publish over 3 future bookings with enough seats (each gets a distinct seat) and over a day with more bookings than seats (publish rejected, nothing changed).

### Section 2: Error & Rescue Map

```
  METHOD/CODEPATH                    | WHAT CAN GO WRONG                                   | ERROR CLASS
  -----------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|---------------------------
  PlanService.Choose (activation)    | plan missing/invalid in request                     | ValidationError (422)
                                     | payment method add fails                            | PaymentMethodError
  PlanService.ScheduleSwitch         | double submit / two tabs                            | UniqueViolation (tenant,starts_on)
                                     | caller not Owner                                    | Forbidden (403)
  PlanService.CancelSwitch           | cancel on/after the 1st it starts                   | PlanAlreadyStarted (409)
  MonthlyRating.RateTenant (by plan) | no plan period for an Active/PastDue/Restricted day | PlanPeriodMissing
                                     | two periods overlap (data bug)                      | ExclusionViolation (prevented)
                                     | rate card version missing for the month             | RateCardMissing (existing)
                                     | metering day incomplete (Usage plan)                | MeteringIncomplete (C2-A2)
  WalletDebit (Fixed, over allowance)| two sends cross the allowance at the same time      | none: atomic count+debit (R4)
                                     | balance at zero                                     | WalletInsufficient -> email fallback (Rule #6)
  SeatMap.Publish                    | unassigned seat, duplicate label, overlapping block | SeatMapInvalid (422, list)
                                     | removes/reassigns a seat with future bookings       | SeatInUse (409, bookings)
                                     | more future bookings than seats on a day (R3-A1)    | SeatShortage (409, days)
                                     | a hold lands on a seat being removed                | race: Section 4 (R3-D1)
  SeatHold (picked seatIds)          | seat taken during OTP                               | SeatTaken (409 + fresh states)
                                     | seatId not a seat of this product                   | ValidationError (422)
  ChangeSeat (staff)                 | target seat taken                                   | SeatTaken (409)
                                     | customer notification fails                         | NotificationFailed (retried)
  SeatAvailability (public)          | map not published / product has no map              | NotFound -> "Any available seat" only

  ERROR CLASS          | RESCUED? | RESCUE ACTION                                           | USER SEES
  ---------------------|----------|---------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------
  ValidationError      | Y        | reject with field errors                                | inline message
  PaymentMethodError   | Y        | stay in Trial                                           | "Payment method not added · try again"
  UniqueViolation      | Y        | return the existing scheduled switch (idempotent)       | "Fixed plan from 1 January"
  PlanAlreadyStarted   | Y        | reject                                                  | "Your new plan already started"
  PlanPeriodMissing    | N <- GAP | (R3-E1)                                                 | (R3-E1)
  RateCardMissing      | Y        | hold rating, alert SSPL (existing)                      | invoice later, with reason
  MeteringIncomplete   | Y        | hold invoice, alert, retry daily (C2-A2)                | invoice later, with reason
  WalletInsufficient   | Y        | SMS/WhatsApp fall back to email (Rule #6)               | owner banner; customer gets email
  SeatMapInvalid       | Y        | reject publish, highlight cells                         | list of problems on the map
  SeatInUse            | Y        | reject publish, list bookings with "Move" links         | "3 seats have future bookings"
  SeatShortage         | Y        | reject publish, list days                               | "12 Nov: 14 bookings, 12 seats"
  SeatTaken            | Y        | release the hold attempt, return fresh seat states      | "A3 was just booked · pick another"
  NotificationFailed   | Y        | existing notification retry and delivery log            | staff see delivery status
  NotFound (map)       | Y        | booking app shows "Any available seat" only             | normal booking without a map
```

Finding:
- **GAP R3-E1. Rating a tenant with no plan period.** MonthlyRating now picks the invoice shape from TenantPlanPeriod (FR-PL-060). If an Active, PastDue or Restricted day has no period (a bug in activation or switching), the plan does not say what rating does. Choosing a plan silently would bill the wrong amount.

## Answered: R3-E1 (D8)
Approved: hold and alert ("Hold that invoice and alert SSPL (recommended)", 2026-11-05). If any Active, PastDue or Restricted day of the month has no TenantPlanPeriod, MonthlyRating holds that tenant invoice, alerts SSPL Finance with the tenant and the days, shows the owner "Invoice delayed · we are checking", and retries daily after the period is repaired, exactly as the C2-A2 metering hold. It never guesses a plan. Test: rating with one gap day holds that invoice, raises the alert and still bills other tenants.

### Section 3: Security & Threat Model

| Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| A Manager or Staff user changes the tenant's plan | Low | Medium | Yes: plan change is Owner-only, audited, confirmed by email (FR-TA-182) |
| A customer books another tenant's seat by sending its seatId | Low | High | Yes: RLS scopes every query to the tenant; the hold rejects a seatId that is not a seat of the booked product (ValidationError, Section 2) |
| Public seat availability leaks who is sitting where | Low | Medium | Yes: the public endpoint returns layout, labels, tags and free/booked/out-of-service only, never customer data (10-api-modules) |
| Script injection through seat labels or text-label cells | Medium | Medium | Partly: React renders text escaped; the implementation must also cap lengths (seat label, text label) and reject control characters. Implementation owner must prove both with a test |
| Scraping the public seat endpoint | Low | Low | Yes: existing WAF and per-IP rate limits; short cache per map and time |
| SSPL support edits a map without consent | Low | Medium | Yes: only under an Owner-approved support grant (FR-TA-068d, FR-SA-030) |
| Seat moves and auto-assignments untraceable | Low | Medium | Yes: each move or auto-assignment writes a BookingEvent; map publishes write an AuditEvent |

No High-severity findings. No new secrets or dependencies.

### Section 4: Data Flow & Interaction Edge Cases

```
SEAT HOLD:      picked seat ids -> validate (seat of this product, not archived) -> insert allocation
                (exclusion constraint) -> hold row -> Razorpay order
  shadow: empty list -> "Any available seat" assigns one | stale id from an old map -> 409 + fresh map
          seat taken -> 409 + fresh states | timeout -> hold expires (existing sweep)

MAP PUBLISH:    draft -> validate cells -> check future allocations on removed/reassigned units
                -> auto-assign seatless pool bookings (R3-A1) -> archive removed units -> new version live
  shadow: invalid draft -> 422 list | seats in use -> 409 | shortage -> 409 days
          concurrent hold on a unit being removed -> see R3-D1

PLAN SWITCH:    Owner request -> validate -> insert FUTURE period (unique tenant+starts_on) -> email
  shadow: double submit -> existing row returned | cancel after start -> 409
```

Async ordering (map publish vs a customer's hold on the same seat). Invariant: no booking allocation may point at a seat that a published map removed.

```
  time | Publish (owner)                          | Hold (customer)                       | product_unit A3
  t1   | reads: A3 has no future allocations      |                                       | active
  t2   |                                          | inserts allocation on A3 (FK ok)      | active
  t3   | archives A3, commits                     |                                       | archived
  t4   |                                          | commits                               | archived + live booking  <- violation
```

The FK check only takes a KEY SHARE lock and archiving is a non-key UPDATE, so nothing stops this order today.

| Interaction | Edge case | Handled? | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| Publish map | double click | Yes | publish carries the draft version number; a second publish of the same draft is a no-op |
| Publish map | zero seats but Seat choice on | Gap, determined | Seat choice can be Optional or Required only with at least one active seat; publish validation rejects otherwise |
| Pick seat | page open while owner publishes a new map | Yes | hold returns 409 with the fresh map |
| Pick seat | back button after picking | Yes | no hold exists before OTP (FR-BA-017) |
| Pick seat | 40 × 60 map on a small phone | Yes | zoom and pan; list view; NFR-PERF-06 budget |
| Plan switch | two tabs submit different plans | Yes | unique (tenant, starts_on); the later request gets the scheduled switch back |
| Plan switch | cancel at 23:59 on the last day | Yes | allowed until starts_on in IST; rejected after |

Finding:
- **WARNING R3-D1. A hold can land on a seat that a map publish is removing** (schedule above).

## Answered: R3-D1 (D9)
Approved: row locks ("Lock just the seats being changed (recommended)", 2026-11-05). SeatMap.Publish locks the ProductUnit rows it removes or reassigns with FOR UPDATE, then re-checks their future allocations; seat holds lock the picked or assigned units with FOR SHARE in the hold transaction and reject archived units with SeatTaken (409 + fresh map). Test: controlled pause points, both completion orders; no allocation ever references an archived seat.

### Section 5: Code Quality

- OK. Fixed-plan over-allowance prices go through the one SlabCalculator (eng re-review 3); there must be no second pricing path for Fixed tenants. The "other plan" comparison and the Usage-plan equivalent stored for Fixed tenants (FR-PL-064) call the same calculator.
- OK. Seat labelling (A1, A2...) runs on the server at publish; the builder may preview it, but the server result is the one saved, so the two can't disagree in the database.
- OK. Placement has one source per state: a draft holds placements in SeatMap.draft_layout (eng review 5, D1); publish copies unit placements to ProductUnit in the same transaction (09 data model). Bookings never read the layout.
- WARNING (determined, no new choice): SeatMap.Publish does validation, conflict checks, auto-assignment (R3-A1), locking (R3-D1) and archiving. Split it into named steps (Validate, LockAffectedUnits, CheckFutureAllocations, AutoAssignSeatless, Apply) inside one transaction so no step branches more than 5 ways.

No new choices from this section.
